What a run is. One invocation of the framework on one design with one mode: here conv_layer with mac_27x27_fixed_pt_mode.

What happened. 4 rewrites measured (Pass 1, Pass 2, Pass 3, Pass 4); kept: Pass 3 (the lowest area x delay, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 5.81%). Loop stopped by: no gain beyond noise (gain -10.90% against Pass 3, min-gain 0.0581 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac)).

Result. Area x delay -10.18% against the unmodified design on the same layout (koios_small, 140 x 140): 3.48222e+08 vs 3.87672e+08 (baseline: median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them)); verification: not recorded in the run record.

Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_27x27` (precision INT27)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
- output ports: out[54]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference simulation files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.3
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, 12 PEs x 7 matmuls): clk -> clk; seq_mac.reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (the module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (the module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> a new wire mul_out (the block's running sum, low 32 bits), from which the design's mul_out_reg value is recovered as mul_out - mul_out_prev; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (the single qmult mult_u1 at conv_layer.v:2793 inside seq_mac, instantiated once per processing_element at 2648, 12 live PEs per systolic_pe_matrix (2570-2588) x 7 matmul_4x4_systolic). It is converted. No candidate is left.

Why the product is turned into an accumulation and differenced back (the shape of the rewrite). In seq_mac the 32-bit sum register add_out_reg (2806-2818) is not a plain accumulator: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808) overwrite it with {0,a_flopped} + {0,b_flopped}, and the next non-eltwise cycle resumes accumulating from that overwritten value, with no reset in between (the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add at random and tasks_conv_seq_mac.vh states the verdict build forces no reset when eltwise_mode falls). A block whose out is the design's accumulator would have to take the value a_flopped+b_flopped one cycle before that value exists, which the block cannot do (its out is 2 cycles behind its operand ports, and it has no load). So the architectural accumulator stays in soft logic exactly as written, and the block is used for the product instead: the block accumulates the same product stream, and the single product is recovered as the one-cycle difference of its running sum. That needs no assumption about the design's control and is exact under arbitrary reset, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add.

Edit (all inside module seq_mac, conv_layer.v:2765-2846; nothing else in the file changes).

  1. Delete the qmult mult_u1(...) instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg register declaration (2781) and its always block (2795-2801). Leave module qmult (2849-2857) in the file, now uninstantiated.
  2. Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block (2777-2791) exactly as they are: they are read by add_in1/add_in2, so they must stay outside the block.

  3. Add:

    • wire [53:0] mac_acc;
    • mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}), .b({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}), .out(mac_acc)); — note the block is fed the module inputs a,b, not a_flopped/b_flopped: the block's own input registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped bit for bit (same clk, same synchronous active-high clear to 0 from the same reset).
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out = mac_acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];
    • reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_prev; with always @(posedge clk) if (reset) mul_out_prev <= 0; else mul_out_prev <= mul_out;`
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg = mul_out - mul_out_prev;` (same name, now a wire, so line 2808 and the rest of the module are untouched).
  4. add_in1, add_in2, qadd add_u1, add_out_reg, mac_out and the out register (2804-2844) are unchanged.

Why it is the same behaviour, every cycle. Write R_t for reset sampled at edge t and P_t = a_flopped*b_flopped after edge t. Original: mul_out_reg after edge t = R_t ? 0 : a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1). Block: its operand registers after edge k hold R_k ? 0 : a(k), i.e. exactly a_flopped/b_flopped, so mac_acc after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1) + a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1), and mul_out_prev after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1). Their difference is 0 when R_t (both cleared) and a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1) otherwise — the original mul_out_reg in both cases, exactly, since each 16x16 product is < 2^32 and the low 32 bits of a mod-2^54 running sum are the mod-2^32 running sum, matching qadd's 32-bit wrap. The operands are zero-extended into unsigned 27-bit ports, which is what qmult's unsigned i_multiplicand * i_multiplier does. Every register the rewrite adds (mul_out_prev, and the block's own) is cleared by the design's reset and nothing relies on a power-up value: at the first reset edge mac_acc, mul_out_prev and the difference are all 0, and the cycle after release reproduces the original's 0 as well. No ASSUMES: line is needed; in particular the rewrite does not rely on a reset between an eltwise-add phase and the next MAC phase.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates one block per seq_mac, 84 blocks. Each takes a DSP tile alone, and the baseline already spends one tile per seq_mac on a lone two_mult_18x19, so the design still uses 84 of the device's 306 tiles: no tile is saved, the gain is the pipeline, not the tile count.

Longest series of blocks in a stage. One. No block drives another block: each block's out goes only to soft logic in its own seq_mac. Its stage is the block's internal one, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, against the baseline's slowest stage 5.57582 ns.

Stages the rewrite leaves, and what moves out of which stage.

  • Baseline's slowest stage disappears. The stage a_flopped/b_flopped -> hard multiply (two_mult_18x19) -> one LUT -> mul_out_reg (5.57582 ns, wire 1.63 in and 1.56 out; and its twin $mul~690 at 5.496 ns — both are this same qmult, so converting the region removes all 84 of them) is replaced by three shorter stages: into the block 0.060 + 1.627 (wire, register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns; inside the block 2.219 ns; and out of the block as below. The 32 flip-flops of mul_out_reg leave the fabric in that stage — they become the block's output register.
  • The accumulate stage gets one level deeper and becomes the slowest stage the rewrite leaves: mac_acc/mul_out_prev -> 32-bit difference -> the existing add_in2 mux and qadd -> add_out_reg: 0.060 (block out clock-to-Q) + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic, median) + 1.14 (soft adder level, the difference) + 1.14 (the mux folded into the adder's input LUTs plus the 32-bit qadd) ~= 3.3 ns. This is ~1.14 ns (one soft adder level) longer than the baseline's accumulate stage, which is against the cost guidance; the reason is that this is the price of the exactness argued above, and 3.3 ns is still well under the 5.57582 ns stage the rewrite deletes, so it cannot set the period. No soft adder leaves the design: qadd and add_out_reg stay soft because the eltwise-add overwrite forbids moving them into the block.
  • Unchanged stages: a,b into a_flopped/b_flopped, and add_out_reg -> mac_out (down-cast) -> out. The down-cast stage (a 15-bit OR reduce and a mux, ~3 soft levels, ~4.4 ns by the table's medians) is untouched and is the likely new critical path, so the expected period is roughly 4.4-4.5 ns rather than 5.576.
  • Cost expectation: flip-flops are a wash (mul_out_prev replaces mul_out_reg one for one); the added soft logic is one 32-bit subtract per instance (84 x ~16-32 BLEs), a few percent of the 6.1312e7 area, against a ~20% cut in the critical path, so area x delay x wirelength is expected to fall.

Register stages, operands to result, for the converted region. Before: a,b -> stage 1 a_flopped/b_flopped -> multiply -> stage 2 mul_out_reg -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg (the product is registered 1 stage before the sum). After: a,b -> stage 1 the block's input registers -> multiply and add into the block's running sum -> stage 2 the block's out register -> difference (combinational) -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg. Identical count and identical arrival cycle for every term; the product still reaches the sum's adder one register stage before the sum is registered.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add path did not overwrite the accumulator add_out_reg in place (for example a separate output path for a_flopped+b_flopped, or a guaranteed reset whenever eltwise_mode falls), the whole accumulation could move into the block: out[31:0] would be the accumulator directly, and the soft qadd, its input muxes, mul_out_prev and the differencing subtract would all disappear, making the rewrite an area win as well as a delay win.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_27x27.v"

output
1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference simulation files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (the single qmult mult_u1 at conv_layer.v:2793 inside seq_mac, instantiated once per processing_element at 2648, 12 live PEs per systolic_pe_matrix (2570-2588) x 7 matmul_4x4_systolic). It is converted. No candidate is left.

Why the product is turned into an accumulation and differenced back (the shape of the rewrite). In seq_mac the 32-bit sum register add_out_reg (2806-2818) is not a plain accumulator: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808) overwrite it with {0,a_flopped} + {0,b_flopped}, and the next non-eltwise cycle resumes accumulating from that overwritten value, with no reset in between (the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add at random and tasks_conv_seq_mac.vh states the verdict build forces no reset when eltwise_mode falls). A block whose out is the design's accumulator would have to take the value a_flopped+b_flopped one cycle before that value exists, which the block cannot do (its out is 2 cycles behind its operand ports, and it has no load). So the architectural accumulator stays in soft logic exactly as written, and the block is used for the product instead: the block accumulates the same product stream, and the single product is recovered as the one-cycle difference of its running sum. That needs no assumption about the design's control and is exact under arbitrary reset, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add.

Edit (all inside module seq_mac, conv_layer.v:2765-2846; nothing else in the file changes).

  1. Delete the qmult mult_u1(...) instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg register declaration (2781) and its always block (2795-2801). Leave module qmult (2849-2857) in the file, now uninstantiated.
  2. Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block (2777-2791) exactly as they are: they are read by add_in1/add_in2, so they must stay outside the block.

  3. Add:

    • wire [53:0] mac_acc;
    • mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}), .b({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}), .out(mac_acc)); — note the block is fed the module inputs a,b, not a_flopped/b_flopped: the block's own input registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped bit for bit (same clk, same synchronous active-high clear to 0 from the same reset).
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out = mac_acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];
    • reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_prev; with always @(posedge clk) if (reset) mul_out_prev <= 0; else mul_out_prev <= mul_out;`
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg = mul_out - mul_out_prev;` (same name, now a wire, so line 2808 and the rest of the module are untouched).
  4. add_in1, add_in2, qadd add_u1, add_out_reg, mac_out and the out register (2804-2844) are unchanged.

Why it is the same behaviour, every cycle. Write R_t for reset sampled at edge t and P_t = a_flopped*b_flopped after edge t. Original: mul_out_reg after edge t = R_t ? 0 : a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1). Block: its operand registers after edge k hold R_k ? 0 : a(k), i.e. exactly a_flopped/b_flopped, so mac_acc after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1) + a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1), and mul_out_prev after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1). Their difference is 0 when R_t (both cleared) and a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1) otherwise — the original mul_out_reg in both cases, exactly, since each 16x16 product is < 2^32 and the low 32 bits of a mod-2^54 running sum are the mod-2^32 running sum, matching qadd's 32-bit wrap. The operands are zero-extended into unsigned 27-bit ports, which is what qmult's unsigned i_multiplicand * i_multiplier does. Every register the rewrite adds (mul_out_prev, and the block's own) is cleared by the design's reset and nothing relies on a power-up value: at the first reset edge mac_acc, mul_out_prev and the difference are all 0, and the cycle after release reproduces the original's 0 as well. No ASSUMES: line is needed; in particular the rewrite does not rely on a reset between an eltwise-add phase and the next MAC phase.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates one block per seq_mac, 84 blocks. Each takes a DSP tile alone, and the baseline already spends one tile per seq_mac on a lone two_mult_18x19, so the design still uses 84 of the device's 306 tiles: no tile is saved, the gain is the pipeline, not the tile count.

Longest series of blocks in a stage. One. No block drives another block: each block's out goes only to soft logic in its own seq_mac. Its stage is the block's internal one, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, against the baseline's slowest stage 5.57582 ns.

Stages the rewrite leaves, and what moves out of which stage.

  • Baseline's slowest stage disappears. The stage a_flopped/b_flopped -> hard multiply (two_mult_18x19) -> one LUT -> mul_out_reg (5.57582 ns, wire 1.63 in and 1.56 out; and its twin $mul~690 at 5.496 ns — both are this same qmult, so converting the region removes all 84 of them) is replaced by three shorter stages: into the block 0.060 + 1.627 (wire, register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns; inside the block 2.219 ns; and out of the block as below. The 32 flip-flops of mul_out_reg leave the fabric in that stage — they become the block's output register.
  • The accumulate stage gets one level deeper and becomes the slowest stage the rewrite leaves: mac_acc/mul_out_prev -> 32-bit difference -> the existing add_in2 mux and qadd -> add_out_reg: 0.060 (block out clock-to-Q) + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic, median) + 1.14 (soft adder level, the difference) + 1.14 (the mux folded into the adder's input LUTs plus the 32-bit qadd) ~= 3.3 ns. This is ~1.14 ns (one soft adder level) longer than the baseline's accumulate stage, which is against the cost guidance; the reason is that this is the price of the exactness argued above, and 3.3 ns is still well under the 5.57582 ns stage the rewrite deletes, so it cannot set the period. No soft adder leaves the design: qadd and add_out_reg stay soft because the eltwise-add overwrite forbids moving them into the block.
  • Unchanged stages: a,b into a_flopped/b_flopped, and add_out_reg -> mac_out (down-cast) -> out. The down-cast stage (a 15-bit OR reduce and a mux, ~3 soft levels, ~4.4 ns by the table's medians) is untouched and is the likely new critical path, so the expected period is roughly 4.4-4.5 ns rather than 5.576.
  • Cost expectation: flip-flops are a wash (mul_out_prev replaces mul_out_reg one for one); the added soft logic is one 32-bit subtract per instance (84 x ~16-32 BLEs), a few percent of the 6.1312e7 area, against a ~20% cut in the critical path, so area x delay x wirelength is expected to fall.

Register stages, operands to result, for the converted region. Before: a,b -> stage 1 a_flopped/b_flopped -> multiply -> stage 2 mul_out_reg -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg (the product is registered 1 stage before the sum). After: a,b -> stage 1 the block's input registers -> multiply and add into the block's running sum -> stage 2 the block's out register -> difference (combinational) -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg. Identical count and identical arrival cycle for every term; the product still reaches the sum's adder one register stage before the sum is registered.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add path did not overwrite the accumulator add_out_reg in place (for example a separate output path for a_flopped+b_flopped, or a guaranteed reset whenever eltwise_mode falls), the whole accumulation could move into the block: out[31:0] would be the accumulator directly, and the soft qadd, its input muxes, mul_out_prev and the differencing subtract would all disappear, making the rewrite an area win as well as a delay win.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

  • Region/instance count.   • Widths/arithmetic. DWIDTH=16 (line 14); qmult (2849-2857) is the unsigned i_multiplicand * i_multiplier (the signed DW02_mult on 2855 is commented out), so zero-extending 16→27 into the unsigned block ports is faithful, and each product < 2^32 fits the differencing argument.
  • Visibility. a_flopped/b_flopped are read at 2807 and 2808, so they correctly stay in soft logic; the plan feeds the block the module inputs a,b instead and the block's input registers reproduce them exactly (same clk, same synchronous clear). mul_out (2780/2793) and mul_out_reg (2781/2799) have exactly one reader, 2808 — nothing else reads them, so folding the product into the block's accumulator and reconstructing it is sound.
  • Registers/reset. seq_mac.reset is the PE's mac_reset (2646-2648); every register in seq_mac (2783-2791, 2795-2801, 2812-2818, 2838-2844) uses it as a synchronous active-high clear, matching the block model in sim_reference/mac_int_27x27.v. Difference check holds in both branches: at a reset edge both out and mul_out_prev are 0 → 0, matching mul_out_reg<=0; the first edge after release gives 0·0 = 0 as well; otherwise (acc+P)[31:0] - acc[31:0] = P mod 2^32, exact under 54-bit accumulator wrap. No reliance on power-up values; tb_equiv_seq_mac.v asserts reset for 4 cycles and sets cmp only after release (lines 152-156).
  • No ASSUMES needed. Correct: the reconstruction is independent of eltwise_mode/eltwise_add (driven at 1/8 and 3/4 in set_data, lines 88-89) and of the verdict build's lack of a reset at phase boundaries (tasks_conv_seq_mac.vh:1-4). The plan does not keep the architectural accumulator in the block, which is the one shape the eltwise overwrite at 2807-2808 would have broken.
  • Required items. All present: candidates (one, converted, none left); 84 blocks / 84 tiles; longest block chain = 1 at 2.219 ns vs the 5.57582 ns slowest stage; soft logic leaving (the 32 mul_out_reg flops and the multiply, out of the baseline's slowest stage, stated as slowest) and the new slowest remaining stage; register stages before (a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped → mul_out_reg → add_out_reg) and after (block input regs → block out reg → add_out_reg), identical counts and arrival cycles.
  • Scope. Edits confined to seq_mac; qadd, add_out_reg, the down-cast and out (2804-2844) untouched.

One implementation detail the plan leaves implicit: the existing wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out; declaration at 2780 must be replaced (or driven by assign`) rather than re-declared alongside the new initialized wire; behavior is unaffected.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference simulation files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.3
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, 12 PEs x 7 matmuls): clk -> clk; seq_mac.reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (the module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (the module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> a new wire mul_out (the block's running sum, low 32 bits), from which the design's mul_out_reg value is recovered as mul_out - mul_out_prev; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (the single qmult mult_u1 at conv_layer.v:2793 inside seq_mac, instantiated once per processing_element at 2648, 12 live PEs per systolic_pe_matrix (2570-2588) x 7 matmul_4x4_systolic). It is converted. No candidate is left.

Why the product is turned into an accumulation and differenced back (the shape of the rewrite). In seq_mac the 32-bit sum register add_out_reg (2806-2818) is not a plain accumulator: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808) overwrite it with {0,a_flopped} + {0,b_flopped}, and the next non-eltwise cycle resumes accumulating from that overwritten value, with no reset in between (the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add at random and tasks_conv_seq_mac.vh states the verdict build forces no reset when eltwise_mode falls). A block whose out is the design's accumulator would have to take the value a_flopped+b_flopped one cycle before that value exists, which the block cannot do (its out is 2 cycles behind its operand ports, and it has no load). So the architectural accumulator stays in soft logic exactly as written, and the block is used for the product instead: the block accumulates the same product stream, and the single product is recovered as the one-cycle difference of its running sum. That needs no assumption about the design's control and is exact under arbitrary reset, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add.

Edit (all inside module seq_mac, conv_layer.v:2765-2846; nothing else in the file changes).

  1. Delete the qmult mult_u1(...) instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg register declaration (2781) and its always block (2795-2801). Leave module qmult (2849-2857) in the file, now uninstantiated.
  2. Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block (2777-2791) exactly as they are: they are read by add_in1/add_in2, so they must stay outside the block.

  3. Add:

    • wire [53:0] mac_acc;
    • mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}), .b({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}), .out(mac_acc)); — note the block is fed the module inputs a,b, not a_flopped/b_flopped: the block's own input registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped bit for bit (same clk, same synchronous active-high clear to 0 from the same reset).
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out = mac_acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];
    • reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_prev; with always @(posedge clk) if (reset) mul_out_prev <= 0; else mul_out_prev <= mul_out;`
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg = mul_out - mul_out_prev;` (same name, now a wire, so line 2808 and the rest of the module are untouched).
  4. add_in1, add_in2, qadd add_u1, add_out_reg, mac_out and the out register (2804-2844) are unchanged.

Why it is the same behaviour, every cycle. Write R_t for reset sampled at edge t and P_t = a_flopped*b_flopped after edge t. Original: mul_out_reg after edge t = R_t ? 0 : a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1). Block: its operand registers after edge k hold R_k ? 0 : a(k), i.e. exactly a_flopped/b_flopped, so mac_acc after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1) + a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1), and mul_out_prev after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1). Their difference is 0 when R_t (both cleared) and a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1) otherwise — the original mul_out_reg in both cases, exactly, since each 16x16 product is < 2^32 and the low 32 bits of a mod-2^54 running sum are the mod-2^32 running sum, matching qadd's 32-bit wrap. The operands are zero-extended into unsigned 27-bit ports, which is what qmult's unsigned i_multiplicand * i_multiplier does. Every register the rewrite adds (mul_out_prev, and the block's own) is cleared by the design's reset and nothing relies on a power-up value: at the first reset edge mac_acc, mul_out_prev and the difference are all 0, and the cycle after release reproduces the original's 0 as well. No ASSUMES: line is needed; in particular the rewrite does not rely on a reset between an eltwise-add phase and the next MAC phase.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates one block per seq_mac, 84 blocks. Each takes a DSP tile alone, and the baseline already spends one tile per seq_mac on a lone two_mult_18x19, so the design still uses 84 of the device's 306 tiles: no tile is saved, the gain is the pipeline, not the tile count.

Longest series of blocks in a stage. One. No block drives another block: each block's out goes only to soft logic in its own seq_mac. Its stage is the block's internal one, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, against the baseline's slowest stage 5.57582 ns.

Stages the rewrite leaves, and what moves out of which stage.

  • Baseline's slowest stage disappears. The stage a_flopped/b_flopped -> hard multiply (two_mult_18x19) -> one LUT -> mul_out_reg (5.57582 ns, wire 1.63 in and 1.56 out; and its twin $mul~690 at 5.496 ns — both are this same qmult, so converting the region removes all 84 of them) is replaced by three shorter stages: into the block 0.060 + 1.627 (wire, register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns; inside the block 2.219 ns; and out of the block as below. The 32 flip-flops of mul_out_reg leave the fabric in that stage — they become the block's output register.
  • The accumulate stage gets one level deeper and becomes the slowest stage the rewrite leaves: mac_acc/mul_out_prev -> 32-bit difference -> the existing add_in2 mux and qadd -> add_out_reg: 0.060 (block out clock-to-Q) + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic, median) + 1.14 (soft adder level, the difference) + 1.14 (the mux folded into the adder's input LUTs plus the 32-bit qadd) ~= 3.3 ns. This is ~1.14 ns (one soft adder level) longer than the baseline's accumulate stage, which is against the cost guidance; the reason is that this is the price of the exactness argued above, and 3.3 ns is still well under the 5.57582 ns stage the rewrite deletes, so it cannot set the period. No soft adder leaves the design: qadd and add_out_reg stay soft because the eltwise-add overwrite forbids moving them into the block.
  • Unchanged stages: a,b into a_flopped/b_flopped, and add_out_reg -> mac_out (down-cast) -> out. The down-cast stage (a 15-bit OR reduce and a mux, ~3 soft levels, ~4.4 ns by the table's medians) is untouched and is the likely new critical path, so the expected period is roughly 4.4-4.5 ns rather than 5.576.
  • Cost expectation: flip-flops are a wash (mul_out_prev replaces mul_out_reg one for one); the added soft logic is one 32-bit subtract per instance (84 x ~16-32 BLEs), a few percent of the 6.1312e7 area, against a ~20% cut in the critical path, so area x delay x wirelength is expected to fall.

Register stages, operands to result, for the converted region. Before: a,b -> stage 1 a_flopped/b_flopped -> multiply -> stage 2 mul_out_reg -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg (the product is registered 1 stage before the sum). After: a,b -> stage 1 the block's input registers -> multiply and add into the block's running sum -> stage 2 the block's out register -> difference (combinational) -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg. Identical count and identical arrival cycle for every term; the product still reaches the sum's adder one register stage before the sum is registered.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28

30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end

# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
14:`define DWIDTH 16
2793:qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), .o_result(mul_out));
2849:module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference simulation files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.3
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, 12 PEs x 7 matmuls): clk -> clk; seq_mac.reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (the module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (the module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> a new wire mul_out (the block's running sum, low 32 bits), from which the design's mul_out_reg value is recovered as mul_out - mul_out_prev; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (the single qmult mult_u1 at conv_layer.v:2793 inside seq_mac, instantiated once per processing_element at 2648, 12 live PEs per systolic_pe_matrix (2570-2588) x 7 matmul_4x4_systolic). It is converted. No candidate is left.

Why the product is turned into an accumulation and differenced back (the shape of the rewrite). In seq_mac the 32-bit sum register add_out_reg (2806-2818) is not a plain accumulator: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add holds, add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808) overwrite it with {0,a_flopped} + {0,b_flopped}, and the next non-eltwise cycle resumes accumulating from that overwritten value, with no reset in between (the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add at random and tasks_conv_seq_mac.vh states the verdict build forces no reset when eltwise_mode falls). A block whose out is the design's accumulator would have to take the value a_flopped+b_flopped one cycle before that value exists, which the block cannot do (its out is 2 cycles behind its operand ports, and it has no load). So the architectural accumulator stays in soft logic exactly as written, and the block is used for the product instead: the block accumulates the same product stream, and the single product is recovered as the one-cycle difference of its running sum. That needs no assumption about the design's control and is exact under arbitrary reset, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add.

Edit (all inside module seq_mac, conv_layer.v:2765-2846; nothing else in the file changes).

  1. Delete the qmult mult_u1(...) instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg register declaration (2781) and its always block (2795-2801). Leave module qmult (2849-2857) in the file, now uninstantiated.
  2. Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their always block (2777-2791) exactly as they are: they are read by add_in1/add_in2, so they must stay outside the block.

  3. Add:

    • wire [53:0] mac_acc;
    • mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}), .b({{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}), .out(mac_acc)); — note the block is fed the module inputs a,b, not a_flopped/b_flopped: the block's own input registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped bit for bit (same clk, same synchronous active-high clear to 0 from the same reset).
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out = mac_acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];
    • reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_prev; with always @(posedge clk) if (reset) mul_out_prev <= 0; else mul_out_prev <= mul_out;`
    • wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg = mul_out - mul_out_prev;` (same name, now a wire, so line 2808 and the rest of the module are untouched).
  4. add_in1, add_in2, qadd add_u1, add_out_reg, mac_out and the out register (2804-2844) are unchanged.

Why it is the same behaviour, every cycle. Write R_t for reset sampled at edge t and P_t = a_flopped*b_flopped after edge t. Original: mul_out_reg after edge t = R_t ? 0 : a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1). Block: its operand registers after edge k hold R_k ? 0 : a(k), i.e. exactly a_flopped/b_flopped, so mac_acc after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1) + a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1), and mul_out_prev after edge t = R_t ? 0 : mac_acc(t-1). Their difference is 0 when R_t (both cleared) and a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1) otherwise — the original mul_out_reg in both cases, exactly, since each 16x16 product is < 2^32 and the low 32 bits of a mod-2^54 running sum are the mod-2^32 running sum, matching qadd's 32-bit wrap. The operands are zero-extended into unsigned 27-bit ports, which is what qmult's unsigned i_multiplicand * i_multiplier does. Every register the rewrite adds (mul_out_prev, and the block's own) is cleared by the design's reset and nothing relies on a power-up value: at the first reset edge mac_acc, mul_out_prev and the difference are all 0, and the cycle after release reproduces the original's 0 as well. No ASSUMES: line is needed; in particular the rewrite does not rely on a reset between an eltwise-add phase and the next MAC phase.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates one block per seq_mac, 84 blocks. Each takes a DSP tile alone, and the baseline already spends one tile per seq_mac on a lone two_mult_18x19, so the design still uses 84 of the device's 306 tiles: no tile is saved, the gain is the pipeline, not the tile count.

Longest series of blocks in a stage. One. No block drives another block: each block's out goes only to soft logic in its own seq_mac. Its stage is the block's internal one, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, against the baseline's slowest stage 5.57582 ns.

Stages the rewrite leaves, and what moves out of which stage.

  • Baseline's slowest stage disappears. The stage a_flopped/b_flopped -> hard multiply (two_mult_18x19) -> one LUT -> mul_out_reg (5.57582 ns, wire 1.63 in and 1.56 out; and its twin $mul~690 at 5.496 ns — both are this same qmult, so converting the region removes all 84 of them) is replaced by three shorter stages: into the block 0.060 + 1.627 (wire, register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns; inside the block 2.219 ns; and out of the block as below. The 32 flip-flops of mul_out_reg leave the fabric in that stage — they become the block's output register.
  • The accumulate stage gets one level deeper and becomes the slowest stage the rewrite leaves: mac_acc/mul_out_prev -> 32-bit difference -> the existing add_in2 mux and qadd -> add_out_reg: 0.060 (block out clock-to-Q) + 0.96 (wire, block into soft logic, median) + 1.14 (soft adder level, the difference) + 1.14 (the mux folded into the adder's input LUTs plus the 32-bit qadd) ~= 3.3 ns. This is ~1.14 ns (one soft adder level) longer than the baseline's accumulate stage, which is against the cost guidance; the reason is that this is the price of the exactness argued above, and 3.3 ns is still well under the 5.57582 ns stage the rewrite deletes, so it cannot set the period. No soft adder leaves the design: qadd and add_out_reg stay soft because the eltwise-add overwrite forbids moving them into the block.
  • Unchanged stages: a,b into a_flopped/b_flopped, and add_out_reg -> mac_out (down-cast) -> out. The down-cast stage (a 15-bit OR reduce and a mux, ~3 soft levels, ~4.4 ns by the table's medians) is untouched and is the likely new critical path, so the expected period is roughly 4.4-4.5 ns rather than 5.576.
  • Cost expectation: flip-flops are a wash (mul_out_prev replaces mul_out_reg one for one); the added soft logic is one 32-bit subtract per instance (84 x ~16-32 BLEs), a few percent of the 6.1312e7 area, against a ~20% cut in the critical path, so area x delay x wirelength is expected to fall.

Register stages, operands to result, for the converted region. Before: a,b -> stage 1 a_flopped/b_flopped -> multiply -> stage 2 mul_out_reg -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg (the product is registered 1 stage before the sum). After: a,b -> stage 1 the block's input registers -> multiply and add into the block's running sum -> stage 2 the block's out register -> difference (combinational) -> qadd -> stage 3 add_out_reg. Identical count and identical arrival cycle for every term; the product still reaches the sum's adder one register stage before the sum is registered.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add path did not overwrite the accumulator add_out_reg in place (for example a separate output path for a_flopped+b_flopped, or a guaranteed reset whenever eltwise_mode falls), the whole accumulation could move into the block: out[31:0] would be the accumulator directly, and the soft qadd, its input muxes, mul_out_prev and the differencing subtract would all disappear, making the rewrite an area win as well as a delay win.

  • Region/scope — only seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2856) changed; the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms everything outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac is identical, and qmult (conv_layer.v:2859-2867) is left in place, now uninstantiated, exactly as the plan states.
  • Ports — all four inputs connected by name at conv_layer.v:2794-2797: .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the module's own reset, the same signal clearing a_flopped/b_flopped at 2782-2790), .a({11'b0, a}), .b({11'b0, b}) with DWIDTH=16 so 27-\DWIDTH = 11 and each is 27 bits. .out(mac_acc) is 54 bits and drives only the mul_out slice (2800), as planned; mac_acc[53:32]` unused.
  • Same behavior — the block's operand registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped bit for bit (same clock, same synchronous active-high clear to 0). So mac_acc after edge t is 0 on reset else mac_acc(t-1) + a_flopped(t-1)*b_flopped(t-1), and mul_out_prev (2803-2809) is 0 on reset else mac_acc(t-1). Their difference at 2811 is 0 under reset and the single product otherwise; low 32 bits of a mod-2^54 running sum are the mod-2^32 running sum, and each 16x16 unsigned product is < 2^32, so the subtraction recovers it exactly — matching the original mul_out_reg. qmult was unsigned (2860-2864), so zero-extension is the right extension.
  • Visibility — a_flopped/b_flopped stay in soft logic because add_in1/add_in2 read them (2817-2818); the only reader of mul_out_reg is 2818, unchanged. No value that another reader needs was moved inside the block.
  • Terms — the product term moved into the block; the accumulate (qadd/add_out_reg, 2814-2828), eltwise muxes, down-cast and output register (2830-2854) are untouched, as the plan's reasoning about the in-place eltwise overwrite requires.
  • Registers/reset — the added mul_out_prev and the block's registers are all cleared by the design's reset; nothing relies on a power-up value, and the reset_between_phases diagnostic passes (sim_run.log:24) with x_count 0. No ASSUMES: line is needed and none is claimed.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_27x27.v"

output
1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
