Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The first pass used the block as a bare multiplier: it recovered the single product by differencing the running sum (mul_out - mul_out_prev), which added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register per instance on top of everything the baseline already had (measured +8.37% area, +19.07% wirelength, product 1.270). Nothing soft moved into the block. This revision instead lets the block be the accumulator it is: the block's running sum replaces mul_out_reg + add_out_reg + the 32-bit qadd accumulate, and the only soft arithmetic left is the one add the block cannot do (the eltwise sum) plus a single shared add/sub that carries the offset between the block's running sum and the design's accumulator. Net per instance against the baseline: 64 flip-flops removed (mul_out_reg, add_out_reg), 48 added (base 32, mac_out_flopped 16), the 32-bit qadd replaced by one 33-bit add/sub, plus a 17-bit eltwise adder. Pass 1's two 32-bit additions of soft logic become roughly one.

Candidate regions. One candidate, converted (T2): qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), all 84 instances. Regrouping, hence T2: the product and the accumulate add that follows it a register stage later are merged into one block, and the register stages are moved (the product register mul_out_reg and the sum register add_out_reg are replaced by the block's operand register and running-sum register, and the down-cast is moved ahead of its register). No candidate is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each replacing the two_mult_18x19 multiply that already sat alone on a tile. The design then uses 84 DSP tiles, the same as the baseline, so no tile is paid for twice.

Why the eltwise path forces the offset register. seq_mac is not a plain MAC: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, add_out_reg is loaded with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of accumulating, and accumulation resumes from that loaded value with no reset in between (slices 4 to 6 drive op to 2'b10 at conv_layer.v:1164,1178,1192,1223,1237,1267 and back to 2'b00 at 1003..1111, and the equivalence testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add freely). The block's running sum has no load and no enable, and its reset also clears its operand registers, so clearing it at a load would silently drop two products. The exact fix is an offset: with sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is the design's next accumulator value, and base = T - acc is invariant except on a load, where it becomes elt_sum - acc. Then T = acc + base and add_out = sel ? elt_sum : acc + base, bit for bit the original add_out. Both of the 32-bit sums needed (base + acc and elt_sum - acc) are needed on disjoint cycles, so they share one adder: on a load cycle the base + acc result is unused (the mux takes elt_sum), which is exactly the cycle the subtract is wanted.

Edit, in seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Keep the module ports, the a_flopped/b_flopped registers and their always block unchanged (they are still read by the eltwise sum, so they stay outside the block). Delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2, the qmult and qadd instances, and add_out_reg and its always block. In their place:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers hold a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the sum of
//the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do
wire [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//the add_out of the original
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Then keep the existing down-cast expression verbatim but read add_out where it read add_out_reg (all six references), and put a 16-bit register after it in place of the 32-bit add_out_reg:

verilog
//the down cast now sits before its register instead of after it, so the 32 bit add_out_reg
//becomes these 16 bits; down casting 0 gives 0, so the reset behaves the same
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    mac_out_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    mac_out_flopped <= mac_out;
  end
end

and the final out always block becomes out <= mac_out_flopped (with the same if (reset) out <= 0). qmult and qadd are left in the file, unused.

Same behavior. Writing X(k) for the value a register holds during cycle k: the block's operand registers follow a_reg' = reset ? 0 : a, the same recurrence as a_flopped, so the product the block adds each edge is exactly the original mul_out, and acc[31:0]' = reset ? 0 : acc[31:0] + mul_out (the operands are zero-extended, the design's multiply is unsigned 16x16 so the product is exact in 32 bits, and the block's wrap at 54 bits does not disturb the low 32). With T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg, the original gives T' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum : T) + mul_out, so base = T - acc satisfies base' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum - acc : base) — the register above — and acc + base = T holds from the first reset onward. Hence add_out above equals the original add_out every cycle, mac_out_flopped(k) = downcast(add_out_reg(k)) (down-casting the reset value 0 gives 0, so the reset cycles agree too), and out is identical on every cycle. out is seq_mac's only output; mul_out_reg and add_out_reg had no other reader, while a_flopped/b_flopped do and are kept outside the block. Every added register has a synchronous clear from the same reset, so none relies on a power-up value.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum (2) -> mac_out_flopped (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, so no value moves in time and no extra latency is introduced; base sits beside stage 3, not in series with it.

Stages and delay. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — each seq_mac has a single block and nothing feeds a block from another block. Its stage is the block's own 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The soft logic that leaves is the stage that was the baseline's slowest: register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop (5.576 ns); its multiply and both its registers are now inside the block, so that stage is gone. The stage the rewrite creates is block running sum -> operand mux -> the 33-bit add/sub -> down-cast LUTs -> mac_out_flopped: 0.060 (out of the block) + about 1.2 (wire into soft logic) + 0.15 (mux LUT) + about 1.2 (wire into the adder) + about 1.0 (33 carry stages, two clusters) + 0.66 + 0.153 (down-cast LUT) + 0.019 setup, about 4.4 ns worst case — longer than the baseline's accumulate stage but about 1.1 ns short of the 5.49 ns the design actually ran at in the first pass, which was set by a control-logic path ($sdff~1636 through $sub~1640/$mux~1642/$add~1646 to $sdff~1635) untouched by this rewrite. That control path is why STAGE_NS is given as 5.49: the rewrite is not expected to move the period much either way, and the gain here is area and wirelength, not delay.

Expectation against the first pass. Per instance this removes a 32-bit flip-flop bank and the 32-bit accumulate adder and adds one 33-bit add/sub, a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit register and 16 flip-flops, where the first pass added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register and removed nothing; the hold mux on base and the eltwise adder are the only genuinely new soft cells. Area should land a few percent over the baseline rather than +8.4%, and wirelength, which tracked logic count closely in the first pass, should follow it down, so the product should fall well below the 1.270 now kept.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac loaded the eltwise sum into its own path instead of into the accumulator register (or if the mac were reset between an eltwise phase and the matmul accumulation that follows, which conv_layer's FSM nearly does already via mac_reset), the offset base, its hold mux, the shared 33-bit add/sub and the 17-bit eltwise adder would all disappear and the block's running sum could be down-cast directly, removing roughly 1.6 cluster of soft logic per instance, about 7% of the design's used logic area.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. conv_layer.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan whose header is below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The first pass used the block as a bare multiplier: it recovered the single product by differencing the running sum (mul_out - mul_out_prev), which added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register per instance on top of everything the baseline already had (measured +8.37% area, +19.07% wirelength, product 1.270). Nothing soft moved into the block. This revision instead lets the block be the accumulator it is: the block's running sum replaces mul_out_reg + add_out_reg + the 32-bit qadd accumulate, and the only soft arithmetic left is the one add the block cannot do (the eltwise sum) plus a single shared add/sub that carries the offset between the block's running sum and the design's accumulator. Net per instance against the baseline: 64 flip-flops removed (mul_out_reg, add_out_reg), 48 added (base 32, mac_out_flopped 16), the 32-bit qadd replaced by one 33-bit add/sub, plus a 17-bit eltwise adder. Pass 1's two 32-bit additions of soft logic become roughly one.

Candidate regions. One candidate, converted (T2): qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), all 84 instances. Regrouping, hence T2: the product and the accumulate add that follows it a register stage later are merged into one block, and the register stages are moved (the product register mul_out_reg and the sum register add_out_reg are replaced by the block's operand register and running-sum register, and the down-cast is moved ahead of its register). No candidate is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each replacing the two_mult_18x19 multiply that already sat alone on a tile. The design then uses 84 DSP tiles, the same as the baseline, so no tile is paid for twice.

Why the eltwise path forces the offset register. seq_mac is not a plain MAC: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, add_out_reg is loaded with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of accumulating, and accumulation resumes from that loaded value with no reset in between (slices 4 to 6 drive op to 2'b10 at conv_layer.v:1164,1178,1192,1223,1237,1267 and back to 2'b00 at 1003..1111, and the equivalence testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add freely). The block's running sum has no load and no enable, and its reset also clears its operand registers, so clearing it at a load would silently drop two products. The exact fix is an offset: with sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is the design's next accumulator value, and base = T - acc is invariant except on a load, where it becomes elt_sum - acc. Then T = acc + base and add_out = sel ? elt_sum : acc + base, bit for bit the original add_out. Both of the 32-bit sums needed (base + acc and elt_sum - acc) are needed on disjoint cycles, so they share one adder: on a load cycle the base + acc result is unused (the mux takes elt_sum), which is exactly the cycle the subtract is wanted.

Edit, in seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Keep the module ports, the a_flopped/b_flopped registers and their always block unchanged (they are still read by the eltwise sum, so they stay outside the block). Delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2, the qmult and qadd instances, and add_out_reg and its always block. In their place:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers hold a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the sum of
//the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do
wire [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//the add_out of the original
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Then keep the existing down-cast expression verbatim but read add_out where it read add_out_reg (all six references), and put a 16-bit register after it in place of the 32-bit add_out_reg:

verilog
//the down cast now sits before its register instead of after it, so the 32 bit add_out_reg
//becomes these 16 bits; down casting 0 gives 0, so the reset behaves the same
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    mac_out_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    mac_out_flopped <= mac_out;
  end
end

and the final out always block becomes out <= mac_out_flopped (with the same if (reset) out <= 0). qmult and qadd are left in the file, unused.

Same behavior. Writing X(k) for the value a register holds during cycle k: the block's operand registers follow a_reg' = reset ? 0 : a, the same recurrence as a_flopped, so the product the block adds each edge is exactly the original mul_out, and acc[31:0]' = reset ? 0 : acc[31:0] + mul_out (the operands are zero-extended, the design's multiply is unsigned 16x16 so the product is exact in 32 bits, and the block's wrap at 54 bits does not disturb the low 32). With T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg, the original gives T' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum : T) + mul_out, so base = T - acc satisfies base' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum - acc : base) — the register above — and acc + base = T holds from the first reset onward. Hence add_out above equals the original add_out every cycle, mac_out_flopped(k) = downcast(add_out_reg(k)) (down-casting the reset value 0 gives 0, so the reset cycles agree too), and out is identical on every cycle. out is seq_mac's only output; mul_out_reg and add_out_reg had no other reader, while a_flopped/b_flopped do and are kept outside the block. Every added register has a synchronous clear from the same reset, so none relies on a power-up value.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum (2) -> mac_out_flopped (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, so no value moves in time and no extra latency is introduced; base sits beside stage 3, not in series with it.

Stages and delay. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — each seq_mac has a single block and nothing feeds a block from another block. Its stage is the block's own 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The soft logic that leaves is the stage that was the baseline's slowest: register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop (5.576 ns); its multiply and both its registers are now inside the block, so that stage is gone. The stage the rewrite creates is block running sum -> operand mux -> the 33-bit add/sub -> down-cast LUTs -> mac_out_flopped: 0.060 (out of the block) + about 1.2 (wire into soft logic) + 0.15 (mux LUT) + about 1.2 (wire into the adder) + about 1.0 (33 carry stages, two clusters) + 0.66 + 0.153 (down-cast LUT) + 0.019 setup, about 4.4 ns worst case — longer than the baseline's accumulate stage but about 1.1 ns short of the 5.49 ns the design actually ran at in the first pass, which was set by a control-logic path ($sdff~1636 through $sub~1640/$mux~1642/$add~1646 to $sdff~1635) untouched by this rewrite. That control path is why STAGE_NS is given as 5.49: the rewrite is not expected to move the period much either way, and the gain here is area and wirelength, not delay.

Expectation against the first pass. Per instance this removes a 32-bit flip-flop bank and the 32-bit accumulate adder and adds one 33-bit add/sub, a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit register and 16 flip-flops, where the first pass added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register and removed nothing; the hold mux on base and the eltwise adder are the only genuinely new soft cells. Area should land a few percent over the baseline rather than +8.4%, and wirelength, which tracked logic count closely in the first pass, should follow it down, so the product should fall well below the 1.270 now kept.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac loaded the eltwise sum into its own path instead of into the accumulator register (or if the mac were reset between an eltwise phase and the matmul accumulation that follows, which conv_layer's FSM nearly does already via mac_reset), the offset base, its hold mux, the shared 33-bit add/sub and the 17-bit eltwise adder would all disappear and the block's running sum could be down-cast directly, removing roughly 1.6 cluster of soft logic per instance, about 7% of the design's used logic area.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source.

APPROVE

The plan matches the source and the contract on every point I checked:

  • Region coverage. One candidate (qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances), converted; "No candidate is left" is stated. 84 blocks, 84 tiles; 12 PEs (conv_layer.v:2570-2587) × 7 matmul instances = 84 seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2648).
  • Mapping and registers. The block's operand registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped (same clk, same synchronous clear at conv_layer.v:2783-2791, reset = the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647). With acc = out[31:0], acc' = acc + a_flopped*b_flopped matches the block model (sim_reference/mac_int_27x27.v:29-38); the 16×16 unsigned product (conv_layer.v:2854, DWIDTH=16 at line 14) is exact and the wrap at 54 bits leaves the low 32 bits equal to the design's mod-2³² arithmetic.
  • Offset derivation. T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg with the eltwise load at conv_layer.v:2807-2808 gives base' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum-acc : base), exactly the register the plan writes; at every reset edge acc=base=T=0, so acc+base=T holds from each reset onward, not only at start-up. The shared 33-bit add/sub computes base+acc (bits [32:1] of {base,1}+{acc,0}) and elt_sum-acc ({elt,1}+{~acc,1}) correctly, and elt_sum_ext is 15+17=32 bits, equal to the original's {16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped}.
  • Down-cast move. downcast(0) = 0 on the positive/no-overflow branch (conv_layer.v:2823-2835), so the reset cycles agree; out(k+1) = mac_out_flopped(k) equals downcast(add_out_reg(k)) including the first cycle after any reset. Latency is 4 stages before and after, as stated.
  • Visibility. mul_out_reg and add_out_reg have no reader outside seq_mac (only out is an output, conv_layer.v:2768); a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the eltwise mux and are correctly kept outside the block.
  • Cited lines. slice_*_op <= 2'b10 at 1164, 1178, 1192, 1223, 1237, 1267 and 2'b00 at 1003-1111 are as cited; eltwise_mode/eltwise_add are driven freely by the testbench (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:88-89), and the 4-cycle start-up reset (lines 154-156) covers the block's and the added registers' unknown power-up.
  • Required items. Longest series of blocks in a stage (one, 2.219 ns) against the baseline's slowest stage (5.576 ns); the soft logic leaving and that its stage was the slowest; per-region register stages before and after; no ASSUMES is needed, and none is claimed.

output
1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The first pass used the block as a bare multiplier: it recovered the single product by differencing the running sum (mul_out - mul_out_prev), which added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register per instance on top of everything the baseline already had (measured +8.37% area, +19.07% wirelength, product 1.270). Nothing soft moved into the block. This revision instead lets the block be the accumulator it is: the block's running sum replaces mul_out_reg + add_out_reg + the 32-bit qadd accumulate, and the only soft arithmetic left is the one add the block cannot do (the eltwise sum) plus a single shared add/sub that carries the offset between the block's running sum and the design's accumulator. Net per instance against the baseline: 64 flip-flops removed (mul_out_reg, add_out_reg), 48 added (base 32, mac_out_flopped 16), the 32-bit qadd replaced by one 33-bit add/sub, plus a 17-bit eltwise adder. Pass 1's two 32-bit additions of soft logic become roughly one.

Candidate regions. One candidate, converted (T2): qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), all 84 instances. Regrouping, hence T2: the product and the accumulate add that follows it a register stage later are merged into one block, and the register stages are moved (the product register mul_out_reg and the sum register add_out_reg are replaced by the block's operand register and running-sum register, and the down-cast is moved ahead of its register). No candidate is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each replacing the two_mult_18x19 multiply that already sat alone on a tile. The design then uses 84 DSP tiles, the same as the baseline, so no tile is paid for twice.

Why the eltwise path forces the offset register. seq_mac is not a plain MAC: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, add_out_reg is loaded with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of accumulating, and accumulation resumes from that loaded value with no reset in between (slices 4 to 6 drive op to 2'b10 at conv_layer.v:1164,1178,1192,1223,1237,1267 and back to 2'b00 at 1003..1111, and the equivalence testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add freely). The block's running sum has no load and no enable, and its reset also clears its operand registers, so clearing it at a load would silently drop two products. The exact fix is an offset: with sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is the design's next accumulator value, and base = T - acc is invariant except on a load, where it becomes elt_sum - acc. Then T = acc + base and add_out = sel ? elt_sum : acc + base, bit for bit the original add_out. Both of the 32-bit sums needed (base + acc and elt_sum - acc) are needed on disjoint cycles, so they share one adder: on a load cycle the base + acc result is unused (the mux takes elt_sum), which is exactly the cycle the subtract is wanted.

Edit, in seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Keep the module ports, the a_flopped/b_flopped registers and their always block unchanged (they are still read by the eltwise sum, so they stay outside the block). Delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2, the qmult and qadd instances, and add_out_reg and its always block. In their place:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers hold a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the sum of
//the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do
wire [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//the add_out of the original
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Then keep the existing down-cast expression verbatim but read add_out where it read add_out_reg (all six references), and put a 16-bit register after it in place of the 32-bit add_out_reg:

verilog
//the down cast now sits before its register instead of after it, so the 32 bit add_out_reg
//becomes these 16 bits; down casting 0 gives 0, so the reset behaves the same
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    mac_out_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    mac_out_flopped <= mac_out;
  end
end

and the final out always block becomes out <= mac_out_flopped (with the same if (reset) out <= 0). qmult and qadd are left in the file, unused.

Same behavior. Writing X(k) for the value a register holds during cycle k: the block's operand registers follow a_reg' = reset ? 0 : a, the same recurrence as a_flopped, so the product the block adds each edge is exactly the original mul_out, and acc[31:0]' = reset ? 0 : acc[31:0] + mul_out (the operands are zero-extended, the design's multiply is unsigned 16x16 so the product is exact in 32 bits, and the block's wrap at 54 bits does not disturb the low 32). With T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg, the original gives T' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum : T) + mul_out, so base = T - acc satisfies base' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum - acc : base) — the register above — and acc + base = T holds from the first reset onward. Hence add_out above equals the original add_out every cycle, mac_out_flopped(k) = downcast(add_out_reg(k)) (down-casting the reset value 0 gives 0, so the reset cycles agree too), and out is identical on every cycle. out is seq_mac's only output; mul_out_reg and add_out_reg had no other reader, while a_flopped/b_flopped do and are kept outside the block. Every added register has a synchronous clear from the same reset, so none relies on a power-up value.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum (2) -> mac_out_flopped (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, so no value moves in time and no extra latency is introduced; base sits beside stage 3, not in series with it.

Stages and delay. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — each seq_mac has a single block and nothing feeds a block from another block. Its stage is the block's own 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The soft logic that leaves is the stage that was the baseline's slowest: register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop (5.576 ns); its multiply and both its registers are now inside the block, so that stage is gone. The stage the rewrite creates is block running sum -> operand mux -> the 33-bit add/sub -> down-cast LUTs -> mac_out_flopped: 0.060 (out of the block) + about 1.2 (wire into soft logic) + 0.15 (mux LUT) + about 1.2 (wire into the adder) + about 1.0 (33 carry stages, two clusters) + 0.66 + 0.153 (down-cast LUT) + 0.019 setup, about 4.4 ns worst case — longer than the baseline's accumulate stage but about 1.1 ns short of the 5.49 ns the design actually ran at in the first pass, which was set by a control-logic path ($sdff~1636 through $sub~1640/$mux~1642/$add~1646 to $sdff~1635) untouched by this rewrite. That control path is why STAGE_NS is given as 5.49: the rewrite is not expected to move the period much either way, and the gain here is area and wirelength, not delay.

Expectation against the first pass. Per instance this removes a 32-bit flip-flop bank and the 32-bit accumulate adder and adds one 33-bit add/sub, a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit register and 16 flip-flops, where the first pass added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register and removed nothing; the hold mux on base and the eltwise adder are the only genuinely new soft cells. Area should land a few percent over the baseline rather than +8.4%, and wirelength, which tracked logic count closely in the first pass, should follow it down, so the product should fall well below the 1.270 now kept.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The first pass used the block as a bare multiplier: it recovered the single product by differencing the running sum (mul_out - mul_out_prev), which added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register per instance on top of everything the baseline already had (measured +8.37% area, +19.07% wirelength, product 1.270). Nothing soft moved into the block. This revision instead lets the block be the accumulator it is: the block's running sum replaces mul_out_reg + add_out_reg + the 32-bit qadd accumulate, and the only soft arithmetic left is the one add the block cannot do (the eltwise sum) plus a single shared add/sub that carries the offset between the block's running sum and the design's accumulator. Net per instance against the baseline: 64 flip-flops removed (mul_out_reg, add_out_reg), 48 added (base 32, mac_out_flopped 16), the 32-bit qadd replaced by one 33-bit add/sub, plus a 17-bit eltwise adder. Pass 1's two 32-bit additions of soft logic become roughly one.

Candidate regions. One candidate, converted (T2): qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), all 84 instances. Regrouping, hence T2: the product and the accumulate add that follows it a register stage later are merged into one block, and the register stages are moved (the product register mul_out_reg and the sum register add_out_reg are replaced by the block's operand register and running-sum register, and the down-cast is moved ahead of its register). No candidate is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each replacing the two_mult_18x19 multiply that already sat alone on a tile. The design then uses 84 DSP tiles, the same as the baseline, so no tile is paid for twice.

Why the eltwise path forces the offset register. seq_mac is not a plain MAC: when eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, add_out_reg is loaded with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of accumulating, and accumulation resumes from that loaded value with no reset in between (slices 4 to 6 drive op to 2'b10 at conv_layer.v:1164,1178,1192,1223,1237,1267 and back to 2'b00 at 1003..1111, and the equivalence testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add freely). The block's running sum has no load and no enable, and its reset also clears its operand registers, so clearing it at a load would silently drop two products. The exact fix is an offset: with sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is the design's next accumulator value, and base = T - acc is invariant except on a load, where it becomes elt_sum - acc. Then T = acc + base and add_out = sel ? elt_sum : acc + base, bit for bit the original add_out. Both of the 32-bit sums needed (base + acc and elt_sum - acc) are needed on disjoint cycles, so they share one adder: on a load cycle the base + acc result is unused (the mux takes elt_sum), which is exactly the cycle the subtract is wanted.

Edit, in seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Keep the module ports, the a_flopped/b_flopped registers and their always block unchanged (they are still read by the eltwise sum, so they stay outside the block). Delete mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2, the qmult and qadd instances, and add_out_reg and its always block. In their place:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers hold a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the sum of
//the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do
wire [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//the add_out of the original
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Then keep the existing down-cast expression verbatim but read add_out where it read add_out_reg (all six references), and put a 16-bit register after it in place of the 32-bit add_out_reg:

verilog
//the down cast now sits before its register instead of after it, so the 32 bit add_out_reg
//becomes these 16 bits; down casting 0 gives 0, so the reset behaves the same
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out_flopped;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    mac_out_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    mac_out_flopped <= mac_out;
  end
end

and the final out always block becomes out <= mac_out_flopped (with the same if (reset) out <= 0). qmult and qadd are left in the file, unused.

Same behavior. Writing X(k) for the value a register holds during cycle k: the block's operand registers follow a_reg' = reset ? 0 : a, the same recurrence as a_flopped, so the product the block adds each edge is exactly the original mul_out, and acc[31:0]' = reset ? 0 : acc[31:0] + mul_out (the operands are zero-extended, the design's multiply is unsigned 16x16 so the product is exact in 32 bits, and the block's wrap at 54 bits does not disturb the low 32). With T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg, the original gives T' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum : T) + mul_out, so base = T - acc satisfies base' = reset ? 0 : (sel ? elt_sum - acc : base) — the register above — and acc + base = T holds from the first reset onward. Hence add_out above equals the original add_out every cycle, mac_out_flopped(k) = downcast(add_out_reg(k)) (down-casting the reset value 0 gives 0, so the reset cycles agree too), and out is identical on every cycle. out is seq_mac's only output; mul_out_reg and add_out_reg had no other reader, while a_flopped/b_flopped do and are kept outside the block. Every added register has a synchronous clear from the same reset, so none relies on a power-up value.

Register stages, before and after. Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum (2) -> mac_out_flopped (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, so no value moves in time and no extra latency is introduced; base sits beside stage 3, not in series with it.

Stages and delay. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — each seq_mac has a single block and nothing feeds a block from another block. Its stage is the block's own 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The soft logic that leaves is the stage that was the baseline's slowest: register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop (5.576 ns); its multiply and both its registers are now inside the block, so that stage is gone. The stage the rewrite creates is block running sum -> operand mux -> the 33-bit add/sub -> down-cast LUTs -> mac_out_flopped: 0.060 (out of the block) + about 1.2 (wire into soft logic) + 0.15 (mux LUT) + about 1.2 (wire into the adder) + about 1.0 (33 carry stages, two clusters) + 0.66 + 0.153 (down-cast LUT) + 0.019 setup, about 4.4 ns worst case — longer than the baseline's accumulate stage but about 1.1 ns short of the 5.49 ns the design actually ran at in the first pass, which was set by a control-logic path ($sdff~1636 through $sub~1640/$mux~1642/$add~1646 to $sdff~1635) untouched by this rewrite. That control path is why STAGE_NS is given as 5.49: the rewrite is not expected to move the period much either way, and the gain here is area and wirelength, not delay.

Expectation against the first pass. Per instance this removes a 32-bit flip-flop bank and the 32-bit accumulate adder and adds one 33-bit add/sub, a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit register and 16 flip-flops, where the first pass added a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit register and removed nothing; the hold mux on base and the eltwise adder are the only genuinely new soft cells. Area should land a few percent over the baseline rather than +8.4%, and wirelength, which tracked logic count closely in the first pass, should follow it down, so the product should fall well below the 1.270 now kept.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac loaded the eltwise sum into its own path instead of into the accumulator register (or if the mac were reset between an eltwise phase and the matmul accumulation that follows, which conv_layer's FSM nearly does already via mac_reset), the offset base, its hold mux, the shared 33-bit add/sub and the 17-bit eltwise adder would all disappear and the block's running sum could be down-cast directly, removing roughly 1.6 cluster of soft logic per instance, about 7% of the design's used logic area.

response

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules:

  • Plan fidelity. The edit touches only seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2863); the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is unchanged. Every item the plan names is present: mul_out/mul_out_reg, add_in1/add_in2, add_out_reg and the qmult/qadd instances are gone; the block, acc, elt_sum, base, the shared add/sub, the re-pointed down-cast and mac_out_flopped are as written. qmult/qadd are left in the file unused, as the plan states.
  • Ports. All four inputs connected by name (conv_layer.v:2794-2797): clk->clk, module reset->reset, a/b zero-extended 16->27 via {{(27-DWIDTH){1'b0}}, …}. out[31:0] drives acc; out[53:32]` unused — the mapping the plan gives.
  • Same behavior. The block's operand registers obey the same recurrence as a_flopped/b_flopped (same clock, same synchronous clear, no enable), so the term added each edge is exactly the original mul_out; the design's multiply is unsigned 16x16, so the product is exact and the wrap at 2^54 does not disturb acc[31:0]. With T = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg, base' = sel ? elt_sum-acc : base is exactly T'-acc', which is what conv_layer.v:2819-2825 implements, so add_out at 2817 equals the original add_out bit for bit each cycle after a reset, including the mid-eltwise load cycles where accumulation resumes from the loaded value. The shared add/sub at 2812-2815 yields base+acc when sel is low and elt_sum_ext + ~acc + 1 when high. The 17-bit elt_sum zero-extended to 32 matches the original 32-bit {16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped} (no carry past bit 16 is possible).
  • Register stages. Four before and after; base sits beside stage 3, not in series. Moving the down-cast ahead of its register is exact because down-casting 0 gives 0, so the reset cycles agree. out timing is unchanged.
  • Visibility/terms. a_flopped/b_flopped are still read by the eltwise sum and were kept outside the block; mul_out_reg/add_out_reg had no other reader, and out is seq_mac's only output (conv_layer.v:2648 is the sole instantiation site, reading only out). No term is dropped.
  • Reset/power-up. Block reset is driven by the design's own reset, so every design reset clears the block and every added register (base, mac_out_flopped) has a synchronous clear from the same signal; nothing relies on a power-up value. The mid-run reset diagnostic passes (sim_run.log:24).
  • No ASSUMES: is needed, and none is claimed.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
