Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept revision's rewrite with its one mistake undone: the kept revision deleted the 32-bit add_out_reg and moved the down cast ahead of a new 16-bit register, which put the eltwise adder, the 32-bit adder and the four LUT levels of the down cast into a single stage (measured 6.85 ns at seed 1, the whole period). The arithmetic below is unchanged from the kept revision — same block, same base offset, same shared adder, same simulation-passing function — but the register goes back where the original had it: add_out_reg registers the adder, and the down cast sits after it driving out, exactly as in the unmodified source. That splits the 6.85 ns stage into roughly 3.5 ns and 3.5 ns and costs nothing in blocks or tiles.

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances: converted, all 84. It is the only candidate; nothing is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. Each takes a DSP tile alone, so the design uses 84 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline (the baseline's 84 two_mult_18x19 instances each sat alone on a tile as well, the packer having paired none). The block is paid for by what leaves soft logic, not by tile count: per instance the 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply's soft remainder go away, and the measured effect of the kept revision — same block count, same tile count — was area 6.1312e+07 -> 5.48382e+07 (-10.6%) and wirelength 294638 -> 273021 (-7.3%). This revision keeps both of those (it adds 32 flip-flops of add_out_reg, which pack into the BLEs of the 32-bit adder chain that already exists, and removes the kept revision's 16-bit mac_out_flopped and the pure register-to-register copy into out, so the flip-flop count is a wash or slightly better), and it only changes where one register sits, so area and wirelength should land at or just below the kept revision's.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: each seq_mac's block output goes into soft logic and then into a soft register, and the module output out is a soft register. The block stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite creates, and the soft logic that leaves each.

  1. Upstream register -> block a/b: wire 1.314 to 1.940 (median 1.627 on this design) + 0.019 setup = about 1.65 ns, worst about 1.96. Not the slowest stage. The same source also feeds the new 16-bit a+b adder (see stage 1b).
    1b. Upstream register -> {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b} -> the new elt_sum register: 1.63 wire + a 17-bit carry chain (about 0.54) + 0.019 = about 2.2 ns. This is the one structural change beyond the kept revision's arithmetic: a_flopped/b_flopped are read by nothing but the multiply and the eltwise add (conv_layer.v:2788-2789, 2793, 2807-2808 — I checked every reader; the multiply's copy now lives inside the block), so the pair of 16-bit registers is replaced by one 17-bit register holding their sum. That removes 15 flip-flops per instance and takes the eltwise adder out of the stage below.
  2. Block out -> acc -> the add_y mux -> the shared 32-bit adder -> add_out_reg: 0.060 clock-to-Q + about 1.0 wire into soft logic + one LUT level (the mux, which sits in the adder BLE's own LUT) + a 32-bit carry chain (about 1.1) + 0.019 = about 2.4 ns, worst about 3.4. The soft logic that leaves this stage versus the baseline is the whole 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply; the soft logic that enters it is the two operand muxes and the base register. Not the slowest stage.
  3. add_out_reg -> the down cast -> out: unchanged from the baseline in both logic and register placement. From the kept revision's seed-1 report the four LUT levels of the down cast measured 3.411 ns, so from a register this stage is 0.060 + 3.411 + 0.019 = about 3.49 ns. Not the slowest stage, and it was never the slowest in the baseline either.

So the slowest stage the rewrite itself leaves is about 3.5 ns, far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The design's period will therefore be set by logic the rewrite does not touch. Both of the baseline's two slowest stages were qmult multiplies (5.57582 and 5.496 ns) and both are converted, so every remaining stage is at or below 5.496 ns: STAGE_NS is stated as that bound, 5.49, and the expected period is at or below it. Against the kept revision's 6.85059 ns that is a drop of at least 20%, and the expected product is about 0.894 x 0.986 x 0.927 = 0.82 of the baseline, outside the 5.8% seed band.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a/b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum out, which carries the accumulate the original did at add_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg, now holding base + acc (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, same latency on every cycle; the kept revision's extra mac_out_flopped register is gone, since add_out_reg is back in its original place.

Why the arithmetic is still correct (unchanged from the kept revision, which passed the reference testbench). The block accumulates unconditionally, so its running sum runs one cycle ahead of the original's add_out_reg: in pure mac mode the original's combinational add_out at cycle n equals the block's out at cycle n exactly. The eltwise load, which the block cannot do (no enable, no load), is held in a 32-bit base register carrying the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum: add_out = base + acc normally, add_out = elt_sum on an eltwise-add cycle, and on that cycle base takes elt_sum - acc so the invariant holds from the next cycle on. Both uses share one 32-bit carry chain (they are never needed on the same cycle), the subtract's +1 riding in a 33rd low bit. The block's reset is the module's own reset, which synchronously clears the operand registers and the running sum at the same edge that clears a_flopped/b_flopped, elt_sum, base, add_out_reg and out in the design, and at that point base + acc = 0 = add_out, so the invariant is re-established after every reset, not only at start-up. Every added register has a synchronous clear and no rewrite relies on a power-up value; the block's registers are X until the first reset, which the design's reset supplies. The products are 16x16 unsigned into 32 bits and the block's out wraps at 54, so out[31:0] is the same modulo-2^32 running sum the original 32-bit adder produced. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed.

Instructions for the editor. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) replace lines 2777 through 2818 (the a_flopped/b_flopped declarations and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, add_in1/add_in2, the qadd instance and the add_out_reg always block) with:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum <= 0;
  end else begin
    elt_sum <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Leave lines 2774-2775 (reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;) as they are, and leave lines 2820-2844 (the mac_out down cast reading add_out_reg, and the out always block) exactly as in the unmodified source — do not move the down cast and do not add a second register after it. The qmult and qadd modules stay in the file for their other users; seq_mac simply no longer instantiates them. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the whole base register and the shared 32-bit soft adder exist only because seq_mac's eltwise add loads the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of adding into a cleared accumulator. The PE already holds mac_reset high in eltwise mode until ready_for_eltwise_op (conv_layer.v:2647), so if seq_mac were specified to see reset on the cycle before each eltwise load — or if the eltwise sum were routed to the block's a/b with b = 1 instead of loaded over the accumulator — the offset register, both operand muxes and the 32-bit add would all disappear into the block, leaving only the down cast in soft logic. The reference testbench drives reset, eltwise_mode and eltwise_add independently, so this rewrite cannot assume it.

output
1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept revision's rewrite with its one mistake undone: the kept revision deleted the 32-bit add_out_reg and moved the down cast ahead of a new 16-bit register, which put the eltwise adder, the 32-bit adder and the four LUT levels of the down cast into a single stage (measured 6.85 ns at seed 1, the whole period). The arithmetic below is unchanged from the kept revision — same block, same base offset, same shared adder, same simulation-passing function — but the register goes back where the original had it: add_out_reg registers the adder, and the down cast sits after it driving out, exactly as in the unmodified source. That splits the 6.85 ns stage into roughly 3.5 ns and 3.5 ns and costs nothing in blocks or tiles.

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances: converted, all 84. It is the only candidate; nothing is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. Each takes a DSP tile alone, so the design uses 84 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline (the baseline's 84 two_mult_18x19 instances each sat alone on a tile as well, the packer having paired none). The block is paid for by what leaves soft logic, not by tile count: per instance the 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply's soft remainder go away, and the measured effect of the kept revision — same block count, same tile count — was area 6.1312e+07 -> 5.48382e+07 (-10.6%) and wirelength 294638 -> 273021 (-7.3%). This revision keeps both of those (it adds 32 flip-flops of add_out_reg, which pack into the BLEs of the 32-bit adder chain that already exists, and removes the kept revision's 16-bit mac_out_flopped and the pure register-to-register copy into out, so the flip-flop count is a wash or slightly better), and it only changes where one register sits, so area and wirelength should land at or just below the kept revision's.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: each seq_mac's block output goes into soft logic and then into a soft register, and the module output out is a soft register. The block stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite creates, and the soft logic that leaves each.

  1. Upstream register -> block a/b: wire 1.314 to 1.940 (median 1.627 on this design) + 0.019 setup = about 1.65 ns, worst about 1.96. Not the slowest stage. The same source also feeds the new 16-bit a+b adder (see stage 1b).
    1b. Upstream register -> {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b} -> the new elt_sum register: 1.63 wire + a 17-bit carry chain (about 0.54) + 0.019 = about 2.2 ns. This is the one structural change beyond the kept revision's arithmetic: a_flopped/b_flopped are read by nothing but the multiply and the eltwise add (conv_layer.v:2788-2789, 2793, 2807-2808 — I checked every reader; the multiply's copy now lives inside the block), so the pair of 16-bit registers is replaced by one 17-bit register holding their sum. That removes 15 flip-flops per instance and takes the eltwise adder out of the stage below.
  2. Block out -> acc -> the add_y mux -> the shared 32-bit adder -> add_out_reg: 0.060 clock-to-Q + about 1.0 wire into soft logic + one LUT level (the mux, which sits in the adder BLE's own LUT) + a 32-bit carry chain (about 1.1) + 0.019 = about 2.4 ns, worst about 3.4. The soft logic that leaves this stage versus the baseline is the whole 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply; the soft logic that enters it is the two operand muxes and the base register. Not the slowest stage.
  3. add_out_reg -> the down cast -> out: unchanged from the baseline in both logic and register placement. From the kept revision's seed-1 report the four LUT levels of the down cast measured 3.411 ns, so from a register this stage is 0.060 + 3.411 + 0.019 = about 3.49 ns. Not the slowest stage, and it was never the slowest in the baseline either.

So the slowest stage the rewrite itself leaves is about 3.5 ns, far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The design's period will therefore be set by logic the rewrite does not touch. Both of the baseline's two slowest stages were qmult multiplies (5.57582 and 5.496 ns) and both are converted, so every remaining stage is at or below 5.496 ns: STAGE_NS is stated as that bound, 5.49, and the expected period is at or below it. Against the kept revision's 6.85059 ns that is a drop of at least 20%, and the expected product is about 0.894 x 0.986 x 0.927 = 0.82 of the baseline, outside the 5.8% seed band.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a/b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum out, which carries the accumulate the original did at add_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg, now holding base + acc (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, same latency on every cycle; the kept revision's extra mac_out_flopped register is gone, since add_out_reg is back in its original place.

Why the arithmetic is still correct (unchanged from the kept revision, which passed the reference testbench). The block accumulates unconditionally, so its running sum runs one cycle ahead of the original's add_out_reg: in pure mac mode the original's combinational add_out at cycle n equals the block's out at cycle n exactly. The eltwise load, which the block cannot do (no enable, no load), is held in a 32-bit base register carrying the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum: add_out = base + acc normally, add_out = elt_sum on an eltwise-add cycle, and on that cycle base takes elt_sum - acc so the invariant holds from the next cycle on. Both uses share one 32-bit carry chain (they are never needed on the same cycle), the subtract's +1 riding in a 33rd low bit. The block's reset is the module's own reset, which synchronously clears the operand registers and the running sum at the same edge that clears a_flopped/b_flopped, elt_sum, base, add_out_reg and out in the design, and at that point base + acc = 0 = add_out, so the invariant is re-established after every reset, not only at start-up. Every added register has a synchronous clear and no rewrite relies on a power-up value; the block's registers are X until the first reset, which the design's reset supplies. The products are 16x16 unsigned into 32 bits and the block's out wraps at 54, so out[31:0] is the same modulo-2^32 running sum the original 32-bit adder produced. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed.

Instructions for the editor. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) replace lines 2777 through 2818 (the a_flopped/b_flopped declarations and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, add_in1/add_in2, the qadd instance and the add_out_reg always block) with:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum <= 0;
  end else begin
    elt_sum <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Leave lines 2774-2775 (reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;) as they are, and leave lines 2820-2844 (the mac_out down cast reading add_out_reg, and the out always block) exactly as in the unmodified source — do not move the down cast and do not add a second register after it. The qmult and qadd modules stay in the file for their other users; seq_mac simply no longer instantiates them. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the whole base register and the shared 32-bit soft adder exist only because seq_mac's eltwise add loads the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of adding into a cleared accumulator. The PE already holds mac_reset high in eltwise mode until ready_for_eltwise_op (conv_layer.v:2647), so if seq_mac were specified to see reset on the cycle before each eltwise load — or if the eltwise sum were routed to the block's a/b with b = 1 instead of loaded over the accumulator — the offset register, both operand muxes and the 32-bit add would all disappear into the block, leaving only the down cast in soft logic. The reference testbench drives reset, eltwise_mode and eltwise_add independently, so this rewrite cannot assume it.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

APPROVE

Checks performed against conv_layer.v:

  • Readers of a_flopped/b_flopped: only conv_layer.v:2793 (the qmult) and conv_layer.v:2807-2808 (the eltwise mux legs). Nothing outside seq_mac reads them, so merging the two 16-bit registers into the 17-bit elt_sum register preserves every reader; {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} registered equals a_flopped+b_flopped on every cycle including the reset edge (both clear to 0).
  • Accumulator algebra: with M_n = mul_out_reg after edge n, the block's out satisfies A_n = A_{n-1} + M_n while the original's add_out_reg satisfies R_n = R_{n-1} + M_{n-1}, so A_n = R_n + M_n = the original's combinational add_out in mac mode. The base offset with base <= elt_sum - acc on an eltwise-add cycle restores the invariant from the next cycle; base holds otherwise, which is exactly what the invariant requires. The shared adder ({add_x,1'b1} + {add_y,eltwise_add_mode}, taking bits [32:1]) gives base+acc and elt_sum-acc correctly, mod 2^32, matching the original 32-bit qadd.
  • Reset: the block's reset is seq_mac's reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), clearing the operand registers and running sum on the same edge that clears elt_sum, base, add_out_reg and out; at that point base+acc = 0 = add_out, so the invariant re-establishes after every mid-run reset, not just at start-up. Post-reset M = 0 on both sides. No added register relies on a power-up value; the TB asserts reset for RESET_CYCLES before comparing.
  • Latency: 4 register stages before (a_flopped → mul_out_reg → add_out_reg → out) and 4 after (block operand regs → block running sum → add_out_reg → out), same cycle-by-cycle output. Keeping add_out_reg in its original place leaves the downcast (2820-2844) untouched.
  • Widths/arithmetic: operands are unsigned 16-bit, product fits in 32 bits, {11'b0, a}/{11'b0, b} is the right 16→27 zero-extension, and out[31:0] of the mod-2^54 running sum is the mod-2^32 sum the original adder produced.
  • Required items: all candidates accounted for (single candidate, all 84 converted), 84 blocks / 84 tiles, longest block chain of 1 with its delay against the slowest stage, per-stage account of what soft logic leaves and whether that stage is slowest, and before/after register stages. No ASSUMES: is needed and none is claimed.

One inaccuracy that changes nothing in the edit: qmult and qadd have no other instantiations in the file (only conv_layer.v:2793 and 2810), so "stay in the file for their other users" is wrong — leaving the unused module definitions is still harmless and matches the scope rule.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Call 11 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 20 · out 7832 · reasoning 3112 · cache read 221154 · cache write 21839 · 109.0 s
prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept revision's rewrite with its one mistake undone: the kept revision deleted the 32-bit add_out_reg and moved the down cast ahead of a new 16-bit register, which put the eltwise adder, the 32-bit adder and the four LUT levels of the down cast into a single stage (measured 6.85 ns at seed 1, the whole period). The arithmetic below is unchanged from the kept revision — same block, same base offset, same shared adder, same simulation-passing function — but the register goes back where the original had it: add_out_reg registers the adder, and the down cast sits after it driving out, exactly as in the unmodified source. That splits the 6.85 ns stage into roughly 3.5 ns and 3.5 ns and costs nothing in blocks or tiles.

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances: converted, all 84. It is the only candidate; nothing is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. Each takes a DSP tile alone, so the design uses 84 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline (the baseline's 84 two_mult_18x19 instances each sat alone on a tile as well, the packer having paired none). The block is paid for by what leaves soft logic, not by tile count: per instance the 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply's soft remainder go away, and the measured effect of the kept revision — same block count, same tile count — was area 6.1312e+07 -> 5.48382e+07 (-10.6%) and wirelength 294638 -> 273021 (-7.3%). This revision keeps both of those (it adds 32 flip-flops of add_out_reg, which pack into the BLEs of the 32-bit adder chain that already exists, and removes the kept revision's 16-bit mac_out_flopped and the pure register-to-register copy into out, so the flip-flop count is a wash or slightly better), and it only changes where one register sits, so area and wirelength should land at or just below the kept revision's.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: each seq_mac's block output goes into soft logic and then into a soft register, and the module output out is a soft register. The block stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite creates, and the soft logic that leaves each.

  1. Upstream register -> block a/b: wire 1.314 to 1.940 (median 1.627 on this design) + 0.019 setup = about 1.65 ns, worst about 1.96. Not the slowest stage. The same source also feeds the new 16-bit a+b adder (see stage 1b).
    1b. Upstream register -> {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b} -> the new elt_sum register: 1.63 wire + a 17-bit carry chain (about 0.54) + 0.019 = about 2.2 ns. This is the one structural change beyond the kept revision's arithmetic: a_flopped/b_flopped are read by nothing but the multiply and the eltwise add (conv_layer.v:2788-2789, 2793, 2807-2808 — I checked every reader; the multiply's copy now lives inside the block), so the pair of 16-bit registers is replaced by one 17-bit register holding their sum. That removes 15 flip-flops per instance and takes the eltwise adder out of the stage below.
  2. Block out -> acc -> the add_y mux -> the shared 32-bit adder -> add_out_reg: 0.060 clock-to-Q + about 1.0 wire into soft logic + one LUT level (the mux, which sits in the adder BLE's own LUT) + a 32-bit carry chain (about 1.1) + 0.019 = about 2.4 ns, worst about 3.4. The soft logic that leaves this stage versus the baseline is the whole 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply; the soft logic that enters it is the two operand muxes and the base register. Not the slowest stage.
  3. add_out_reg -> the down cast -> out: unchanged from the baseline in both logic and register placement. From the kept revision's seed-1 report the four LUT levels of the down cast measured 3.411 ns, so from a register this stage is 0.060 + 3.411 + 0.019 = about 3.49 ns. Not the slowest stage, and it was never the slowest in the baseline either.

So the slowest stage the rewrite itself leaves is about 3.5 ns, far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The design's period will therefore be set by logic the rewrite does not touch. Both of the baseline's two slowest stages were qmult multiplies (5.57582 and 5.496 ns) and both are converted, so every remaining stage is at or below 5.496 ns: STAGE_NS is stated as that bound, 5.49, and the expected period is at or below it. Against the kept revision's 6.85059 ns that is a drop of at least 20%, and the expected product is about 0.894 x 0.986 x 0.927 = 0.82 of the baseline, outside the 5.8% seed band.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a/b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum out, which carries the accumulate the original did at add_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg, now holding base + acc (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, same latency on every cycle; the kept revision's extra mac_out_flopped register is gone, since add_out_reg is back in its original place.

Why the arithmetic is still correct (unchanged from the kept revision, which passed the reference testbench). The block accumulates unconditionally, so its running sum runs one cycle ahead of the original's add_out_reg: in pure mac mode the original's combinational add_out at cycle n equals the block's out at cycle n exactly. The eltwise load, which the block cannot do (no enable, no load), is held in a 32-bit base register carrying the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum: add_out = base + acc normally, add_out = elt_sum on an eltwise-add cycle, and on that cycle base takes elt_sum - acc so the invariant holds from the next cycle on. Both uses share one 32-bit carry chain (they are never needed on the same cycle), the subtract's +1 riding in a 33rd low bit. The block's reset is the module's own reset, which synchronously clears the operand registers and the running sum at the same edge that clears a_flopped/b_flopped, elt_sum, base, add_out_reg and out in the design, and at that point base + acc = 0 = add_out, so the invariant is re-established after every reset, not only at start-up. Every added register has a synchronous clear and no rewrite relies on a power-up value; the block's registers are X until the first reset, which the design's reset supplies. The products are 16x16 unsigned into 32 bits and the block's out wraps at 54, so out[31:0] is the same modulo-2^32 running sum the original 32-bit adder produced. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed.

Instructions for the editor. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) replace lines 2777 through 2818 (the a_flopped/b_flopped declarations and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, add_in1/add_in2, the qadd instance and the add_out_reg always block) with:

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of the products this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum <= 0;
  end else begin
    elt_sum <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};

//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;

//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

Leave lines 2774-2775 (reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;) as they are, and leave lines 2820-2844 (the mac_out down cast reading add_out_reg, and the out always block) exactly as in the unmodified source — do not move the down cast and do not add a second register after it. The qmult and qadd modules stay in the file for their other users; seq_mac simply no longer instantiates them. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 5.49
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them): clk -> clk; the module input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> new wire acc, the running sum of the products, which stands in for the original's add_out_reg + mul_out_reg; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is the kept revision's rewrite with its one mistake undone: the kept revision deleted the 32-bit add_out_reg and moved the down cast ahead of a new 16-bit register, which put the eltwise adder, the 32-bit adder and the four LUT levels of the down cast into a single stage (measured 6.85 ns at seed 1, the whole period). The arithmetic below is unchanged from the kept revision — same block, same base offset, same shared adder, same simulation-passing function — but the register goes back where the original had it: add_out_reg registers the adder, and the down cast sits after it driving out, exactly as in the unmodified source. That splits the 6.85 ns stage into roughly 3.5 ns and 3.5 ns and costs nothing in blocks or tiles.

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances: converted, all 84. It is the only candidate; nothing is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. Each takes a DSP tile alone, so the design uses 84 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline (the baseline's 84 two_mult_18x19 instances each sat alone on a tile as well, the packer having paired none). The block is paid for by what leaves soft logic, not by tile count: per instance the 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply's soft remainder go away, and the measured effect of the kept revision — same block count, same tile count — was area 6.1312e+07 -> 5.48382e+07 (-10.6%) and wirelength 294638 -> 273021 (-7.3%). This revision keeps both of those (it adds 32 flip-flops of add_out_reg, which pack into the BLEs of the 32-bit adder chain that already exists, and removes the kept revision's 16-bit mac_out_flopped and the pure register-to-register copy into out, so the flip-flop count is a wash or slightly better), and it only changes where one register sits, so area and wirelength should land at or just below the kept revision's.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: each seq_mac's block output goes into soft logic and then into a soft register, and the module output out is a soft register. The block stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns critical path.

Stages the rewrite creates, and the soft logic that leaves each.

  1. Upstream register -> block a/b: wire 1.314 to 1.940 (median 1.627 on this design) + 0.019 setup = about 1.65 ns, worst about 1.96. Not the slowest stage. The same source also feeds the new 16-bit a+b adder (see stage 1b).
    1b. Upstream register -> {1'b0,a} + {1'b0,b} -> the new elt_sum register: 1.63 wire + a 17-bit carry chain (about 0.54) + 0.019 = about 2.2 ns. This is the one structural change beyond the kept revision's arithmetic: a_flopped/b_flopped are read by nothing but the multiply and the eltwise add (conv_layer.v:2788-2789, 2793, 2807-2808 — I checked every reader; the multiply's copy now lives inside the block), so the pair of 16-bit registers is replaced by one 17-bit register holding their sum. That removes 15 flip-flops per instance and takes the eltwise adder out of the stage below.
  2. Block out -> acc -> the add_y mux -> the shared 32-bit adder -> add_out_reg: 0.060 clock-to-Q + about 1.0 wire into soft logic + one LUT level (the mux, which sits in the adder BLE's own LUT) + a 32-bit carry chain (about 1.1) + 0.019 = about 2.4 ns, worst about 3.4. The soft logic that leaves this stage versus the baseline is the whole 32-bit mul_out_reg and the multiply; the soft logic that enters it is the two operand muxes and the base register. Not the slowest stage.
  3. add_out_reg -> the down cast -> out: unchanged from the baseline in both logic and register placement. From the kept revision's seed-1 report the four LUT levels of the down cast measured 3.411 ns, so from a register this stage is 0.060 + 3.411 + 0.019 = about 3.49 ns. Not the slowest stage, and it was never the slowest in the baseline either.

So the slowest stage the rewrite itself leaves is about 3.5 ns, far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The design's period will therefore be set by logic the rewrite does not touch. Both of the baseline's two slowest stages were qmult multiplies (5.57582 and 5.496 ns) and both are converted, so every remaining stage is at or below 5.496 ns: STAGE_NS is stated as that bound, 5.49, and the expected period is at or below it. Against the kept revision's 6.85059 ns that is a drop of at least 20%, and the expected product is about 0.894 x 0.986 x 0.927 = 0.82 of the baseline, outside the 5.8% seed band.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4). After: a/b -> the block's operand registers (1) -> the block's running sum out, which carries the accumulate the original did at add_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg, now holding base + acc (3) -> out (4). Four stages before and after, same latency on every cycle; the kept revision's extra mac_out_flopped register is gone, since add_out_reg is back in its original place.

Why the arithmetic is still correct (unchanged from the kept revision, which passed the reference testbench). The block accumulates unconditionally, so its running sum runs one cycle ahead of the original's add_out_reg: in pure mac mode the original's combinational add_out at cycle n equals the block's out at cycle n exactly. The eltwise load, which the block cannot do (no enable, no load), is held in a 32-bit base register carrying the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum: add_out = base + acc normally, add_out = elt_sum on an eltwise-add cycle, and on that cycle base takes elt_sum - acc so the invariant holds from the next cycle on. Both uses share one 32-bit carry chain (they are never needed on the same cycle), the subtract's +1 riding in a 33rd low bit. The block's reset is the module's own reset, which synchronously clears the operand registers and the running sum at the same edge that clears a_flopped/b_flopped, elt_sum, base, add_out_reg and out in the design, and at that point base + acc = 0 = add_out, so the invariant is re-established after every reset, not only at start-up. Every added register has a synchronous clear and no rewrite relies on a power-up value; the block's registers are X until the first reset, which the design's reset supplies. The products are 16x16 unsigned into 32 bits and the block's out wraps at 54, so out[31:0] is the same modulo-2^32 running sum the original 32-bit adder produced. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed.

Instructions for the editor. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) replace lines 2777 through 2818 (the a_flopped/b_flopped declarations and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, add_in1/add_in2, the qadd instance and the add_out_reg always block) with:

verilog

  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin

  end
end

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    base <= 0;
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    base <= add_sum;
  end
end

The qmult and qadd modules stay in the file for their other users; seq_mac simply no longer instantiates them. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the whole base register and the shared 32-bit soft adder exist only because seq_mac's eltwise add loads the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped instead of adding into a cleared accumulator. The PE already holds mac_reset high in eltwise mode until ready_for_eltwise_op (conv_layer.v:2647), so if seq_mac were specified to see reset on the cycle before each eltwise load — or if the eltwise sum were routed to the block's a/b with b = 1 instead of loaded over the accumulator — the offset register, both operand muxes and the 32-bit add would all disappear into the block, leaving only the down cast in soft logic. The reference testbench drives reset, eltwise_mode and eltwise_add independently, so this rewrite cannot assume it.

response

I'll read the source and the simulation artifacts.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan and the rules:

  • Plan fidelity. The only hunk replaces conv_layer.v:2777-2818 of the original with exactly the code the plan dictated, in the same order. reg out / wire add_out (conv_layer.v:2774-2775) and the down cast plus the out register (conv_layer.v:2831-2855) are untouched — add_out_reg stays in its original place, which is the whole point of this revision over the kept one. The equivalence harness's subtree check confirms nothing outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac moved; qmult/qadd remain as module definitions, merely uninstantiated.
  • Ports. mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk (conv_layer.v:2781-2784) connects all four inputs by name — clk, reset (the module's own reset, i.e. the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647-2648), a and b zero-extended 16→27 — and out[31:0] drives acc, out[53:32] unused, as mapped.
  • Behavior. With the block model's timing (operands at edge k, out at k+1), acc(n) = acc(n-1) + a(n-2)*b(n-2) = acc(n-1) + mul_out_reg(n), so the invariant base + acc = add_out_reg + mul_out_reg holds: it is established at every reset (all of base, acc, add_out_reg, elt_sum clear to 0, and the original's registers are 0 too), preserved on non-load cycles (both sides gain the same product), and restored on an eltwise-add load since base <= elt_sum_ext - acc. add_out equals elt_sum_ext on load cycles, matching {16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped} because elt_sum is 17 bits wide and registered at the same edge. The shared adder is correct both ways: {add_x,1'b1} + {add_y,eltwise_add_mode} yields base+acc with no carry into bit 1, and elt_sum_ext + ~acc + 1 with it. All arithmetic is mod 2^32, and the block's mod-2^54 wrap is consistent on the low 32 bits; the 16×16 unsigned product is exact in both.
  • Visibility and terms. a_flopped/b_flopped were read only by the multiply and the eltwise add inside seq_mac; the multiply moved into the block and the eltwise sum is recomputed outside, so nothing externally visible went inside. Every original term is preserved.
  • Registers/reset. elt_sum, base, add_out_reg all have synchronous clears and nothing relies on a power-up value; the block's X-until-reset registers are cleared by the design's own reset. Latency is unchanged at four register stages.
  • No ASSUMES: is needed — the rewrite holds for arbitrary reset/eltwise_mode/eltwise_add sequences, which is what the testbench drives.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
+wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];
