Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the source and the reference files before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.0
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): clk -> clk; the seq_mac input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> wire acc, the running sum of this mac's products since its last reset; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept candidate (revision 2). The region converted, the block count, the tile count and the arithmetic computed are all unchanged; only the grouping of the soft correction logic changes, to take the late control signal off the carry chain and to delete two 32-bit LUT layers. The resulting seq_mac computes exactly the same function as the kept candidate (shown below by algebra), so it carries the same correctness risk as a candidate that already passed the reference simulation.

Candidate regions

There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances, and it is converted in all 84 instances. Nothing is left.

Why the kept candidate's stage is slow, and what this revision changes

The measured seed-1 path of the kept candidate is: a control register (lo10534) -> 2 LUT levels -> $mux~1192 (the add_x/add_y input mux) -> 33-bit carry chain -> n33606 (the add_out output mux + reset gate) -> $sdff. The control signal eltwise_add_mode reaches the input mux only at 2.857 ns (it comes from the slice's op register through two levels of decode), and the kept candidate then puts a whole carry chain plus a second LUT level behind it: 2.857 + 1.418 (wire) + 0.344 (chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 = 5.447 ns.

The fix is to take eltwise_add_mode out of the adder inputs entirely: use two adders with operands read straight out of registers (no LUT level in front of either chain), and let eltwise_add_mode appear only in the single LUT layer that sits between each chain and its flip-flop. Then the late control signal sees one LUT and a setup (~2.9 ns), and the data sees a chain and one LUT (~2.6 ns), instead of both in series.

The inversion needed for the subtract would otherwise re-introduce a LUT level in front of a chain, so both inverted values are stored inverted: the eltwise sum register holds ~(a+b) and the offset register holds ~(acc - elt_sum). Both inversions are then absorbed free of charge into LUT layers that already exist (the reset gate behind the 17-bit adder, and the two output mux layers), and both chains read acc, a register (nbbar) and a register (elt_sum_n) directly.

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance

Replace the body of seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) between the port/out/add_out declarations and the unchanged down-cast (mac_out, lines 2820-2844) with the following; keep reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;, the mac_out assignment and the out always block exactly as they are. Delete a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2, the qmult mult_u1 instance and the qadd add_u1 instance. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions in the file (they have no other instantiation; synthesis drops them). Change nothing outside seq_mac.

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of every product this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum. It is
//held inverted so the subtract below reads its operands straight out of registers
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum_n;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum_n <= {(`DWIDTH+1){1'b1}};
  end else begin
    elt_sum_n <= ~({1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b});
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ~elt_sum_n};

//nbbar = base - 1, base being this mac's offset from the block's running sum. Holding it
//decremented and inverted lets the sum below carry its +1 in at the bottom of the chain
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nbbar;

//base + acc, the running value. Both operands come from a register or the block, no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   acc_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {nbbar, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_sum     = acc_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//acc - elt_sum, the new offset on a load. ~elt_sum_ext is {1s, elt_sum_n}, again no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   nb_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}, elt_sum_n, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nb_sum     = nb_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : acc_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

//the offset only moves on a cycle that loads the eltwise sum
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    nbbar <= {(2*`DWIDTH){1'b1}};
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    nbbar <= ~nb_sum;
  end
end
Why it computes the original

Write af, bf, mr, ar for the original's a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, S for af+bf, m for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, and index by clock edge.

  • The block's operand registers obey areg(k) = reset(k) ? 0 : a(k-1), the same recurrence as af, because the block's reset is the module's reset and both are synchronous and clear to 0. So areg = af, breg = bf, and acc(k) - acc(k-1) = af(k-1)*bf(k-1) = mr(k) on every non-reset edge; on a reset edge both acc and ar are 0.
  • Invariant: ar(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) (mod 2^32), with base = nbbar + 1. Non-load edge: ar(k+1) = ar(k) + mr(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) + (acc(k)-acc(k-1)) = base(k) + acc(k), which is what acc_sum = acc + nbbar + 1 produces, and nbbar holds still. Load edge (m(k)): ar(k+1) = S_ext(k), taken straight from the mux input elt_sum_ext, and nbbar(k+1) = ~nb_sum(k) = ~(acc(k) - S_ext(k)), so base(k+1) = nbbar(k+1)+1 = S_ext(k) - acc(k) and the invariant holds again. Reset edge: ar = 0, acc = 0, base = 0, elt_sum = 0, and the cycle after reset gives m ? 0 : 0+0 = 0, matching the original, whose mr and ar are also 0 there.
  • ~elt_sum_n is exactly af+bf (17 bits, zero-extended to 32 as in the original {{DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} form); on a reset edge ~{17{1'b1}} = 0, matching the original's cleared af,bf`.
  • nbbar resets to all ones, i.e. base = 0, matching ar = 0 with acc = 0.
  • All arithmetic is unsigned and wraps at 32 bits in both versions; the block wraps at 54 and its low 32 bits are the same residue. The down-cast and out are untouched, and the block's out[53:32] is read by nothing.
  • Power-up: every register the rewrite adds (elt_sum_n, nbbar, add_out_reg) and every register of the block is X until the first reset, and reset clears all of them; nothing relies on an initial value.

This is the same function as the kept candidate, which passed the reference simulation: there add_x+add_y+m is m ? elt_sum - acc : base + acc, add_out = m ? elt_sum : add_sum and base <= m ? elt_sum - acc : base. Here acc_sum = base + acc and ~nb_sum = (elt_sum - acc) - 1 = base_new - 1, with base held as nbbar + 1 and elt_sum held as ~elt_sum_n; the two differ only in gate grouping.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is unconditional, and in particular it does not assume anything about ready_for_eltwise_op or about reset preceding an eltwise load, which the reference testbench drives independently of each other.

Blocks, tiles

84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each taking a DSP tile alone: 84 tiles, the same count as the baseline (where the 84 two_mult_18x19 multiplies each sit alone on a tile, unpaired) and the same as the kept candidate. No tile is saved or spent.

Blocks in series, and the stages

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: a block's out feeds soft logic and a flip-flop, never another block. Its internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the baseline's 5.576 ns critical path, and the path into it (a PE register through in_a/in_b into a/b) is a register-to-first-block wire of 1.314 to 1.940 ns plus 0.019 setup, ~1.96 ns worst, also below.

The stage the rewrite leaves in soft logic, and the one that set the kept candidate's period, is from acc (block out) to add_out_reg/nbbar:

  • data: 0.060 (block clock-to-Q) + ~1.4 (block into soft logic, measured on this design's own routes) + 0.344 (33-bit chain) + 0.660 (wire) + 0.153 (mux + reset-gate LUT) + 0.019 (setup) ≈ 2.64 ns
  • control: the eltwise_add_mode cone reached the kept candidate's mux at 2.703 ns before the mux LUT; it now ends one LUT later, at 2.703 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.87 ns
  • elt_sum_n: PE register 0.060 + ~1.63 (register to this logic) + ~0.30 (17-bit chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.82 ns

So STAGE_NS 3.0, against the kept candidate's measured 5.42 ns and the baseline's 5.576 ns. The kept candidate's path had the late control signal and the chain and a second LUT level in series; this one has the control signal and one LUT level, or the chain and one LUT level, never both. Caveat stated plainly: the design's measured period is the worst stage anywhere in conv_layer, and the two baseline paths I was given (5.576 and 5.496 ns) were both qmult multiply stages that this rewrite removes; if some untouched stage elsewhere in the design sits above 3.0 ns the period will be set there, and the gain will come from area and wirelength alone.

What soft logic leaves which stage

Leaving (per instance, versus the unmodified design): the 16x16 multiply and the 32-bit accumulate add qadd, both now inside the block; a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_reg (96 flip-flops with their reset-gate LUTs); and the add_in1/add_in2 select layers (64 LUTs). Leaving versus the kept candidate: its add_x and add_y layers (64 LUTs, the two 32-bit mux layers that sat in front of the carry chain) — these are the stage that was slowest, so removing them is exactly where the delay comes from.

Entering (per instance): the 17-bit eltwise adder with its 17 reset/invert LUTs and 17 flip-flops; two 33-bit carry chains (~34 adder cells, up from ~17 in the kept candidate); the nbbar register (32 flip-flops) with its 32-LUT select layer; and the 32-LUT add_out select layer. Net against the kept candidate: about 64 fewer LUT-BLEs and about 17 more adder cells per instance, i.e. a smaller stage as well as a shorter one, so area and wirelength should not regress.

Register stages, before and after

Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> qadd -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4).
After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (stage 1) -> multiply and accumulate inside the block -> the block's out (stage 2) -> one soft adder and one LUT -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4). Same number of stages and the same cycle alignment: a product applied at cycle k reaches add_out_reg at edge k+3 in both. elt_sum_n and nbbar are stage-1 and stage-3 registers respectively on the eltwise path, replacing a_flopped/b_flopped and holding the accumulator offset; no stage is added or removed.

DESIGN_CHANGE: processing_element already holds the mac in reset whenever eltwise_mode is high and ready_for_eltwise_op is low (conv_layer.v:2647), so in the design's own use the accumulator is 0 at the first eltwise-add load. If seq_mac were specified so that an eltwise-add load may only occur while the block's running sum is 0 (for instance by gating eltwise_add with the first cycle after reset releases, or by holding mac_reset through the whole eltwise-add burst), the offset would be just the eltwise sum: the second carry chain, the 32-bit nbbar register and its 32-LUT select layer would all disappear, leaving one chain and one LUT layer per mac.

1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output

  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept candidate (revision 2). The region converted, the block count, the tile count and the arithmetic computed are all unchanged; only the grouping of the soft correction logic changes, to take the late control signal off the carry chain and to delete two 32-bit LUT layers. The resulting seq_mac computes exactly the same function as the kept candidate (shown below by algebra), so it carries the same correctness risk as a candidate that already passed the reference simulation.

Candidate regions

There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances, and it is converted in all 84 instances. Nothing is left.

Why the kept candidate's stage is slow, and what this revision changes

The measured seed-1 path of the kept candidate is: a control register (lo10534) -> 2 LUT levels -> $mux~1192 (the add_x/add_y input mux) -> 33-bit carry chain -> n33606 (the add_out output mux + reset gate) -> $sdff. The control signal eltwise_add_mode reaches the input mux only at 2.857 ns (it comes from the slice's op register through two levels of decode), and the kept candidate then puts a whole carry chain plus a second LUT level behind it: 2.857 + 1.418 (wire) + 0.344 (chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 = 5.447 ns.

The fix is to take eltwise_add_mode out of the adder inputs entirely: use two adders with operands read straight out of registers (no LUT level in front of either chain), and let eltwise_add_mode appear only in the single LUT layer that sits between each chain and its flip-flop. Then the late control signal sees one LUT and a setup (~2.9 ns), and the data sees a chain and one LUT (~2.6 ns), instead of both in series.

The inversion needed for the subtract would otherwise re-introduce a LUT level in front of a chain, so both inverted values are stored inverted: the eltwise sum register holds ~(a+b) and the offset register holds ~(acc - elt_sum). Both inversions are then absorbed free of charge into LUT layers that already exist (the reset gate behind the 17-bit adder, and the two output mux layers), and both chains read acc, a register (nbbar) and a register (elt_sum_n) directly.

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance

Replace the body of seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) between the port/out/add_out declarations and the unchanged down-cast (mac_out, lines 2820-2844) with the following; keep reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;, the mac_out assignment and the out always block exactly as they are. Delete a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2, the qmult mult_u1 instance and the qadd add_u1 instance. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions in the file (they have no other instantiation; synthesis drops them). Change nothing outside seq_mac.

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of every product this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum. It is
//held inverted so the subtract below reads its operands straight out of registers
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum_n;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum_n <= {(`DWIDTH+1){1'b1}};
  end else begin
    elt_sum_n <= ~({1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b});
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ~elt_sum_n};

//nbbar = base - 1, base being this mac's offset from the block's running sum. Holding it
//decremented and inverted lets the sum below carry its +1 in at the bottom of the chain
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nbbar;

//base + acc, the running value. Both operands come from a register or the block, no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   acc_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {nbbar, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_sum     = acc_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//acc - elt_sum, the new offset on a load. ~elt_sum_ext is {1s, elt_sum_n}, again no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   nb_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}, elt_sum_n, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nb_sum     = nb_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : acc_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

//the offset only moves on a cycle that loads the eltwise sum
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    nbbar <= {(2*`DWIDTH){1'b1}};
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    nbbar <= ~nb_sum;
  end
end
Why it computes the original

Write af, bf, mr, ar for the original's a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, S for af+bf, m for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, and index by clock edge.

  • The block's operand registers obey areg(k) = reset(k) ? 0 : a(k-1), the same recurrence as af, because the block's reset is the module's reset and both are synchronous and clear to 0. So areg = af, breg = bf, and acc(k) - acc(k-1) = af(k-1)*bf(k-1) = mr(k) on every non-reset edge; on a reset edge both acc and ar are 0.
  • Invariant: ar(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) (mod 2^32), with base = nbbar + 1. Non-load edge: ar(k+1) = ar(k) + mr(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) + (acc(k)-acc(k-1)) = base(k) + acc(k), which is what acc_sum = acc + nbbar + 1 produces, and nbbar holds still. Load edge (m(k)): ar(k+1) = S_ext(k), taken straight from the mux input elt_sum_ext, and nbbar(k+1) = ~nb_sum(k) = ~(acc(k) - S_ext(k)), so base(k+1) = nbbar(k+1)+1 = S_ext(k) - acc(k) and the invariant holds again. Reset edge: ar = 0, acc = 0, base = 0, elt_sum = 0, and the cycle after reset gives m ? 0 : 0+0 = 0, matching the original, whose mr and ar are also 0 there.
  • ~elt_sum_n is exactly af+bf (17 bits, zero-extended to 32 as in the original {{DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} form); on a reset edge ~{17{1'b1}} = 0, matching the original's cleared af,bf`.
  • nbbar resets to all ones, i.e. base = 0, matching ar = 0 with acc = 0.
  • All arithmetic is unsigned and wraps at 32 bits in both versions; the block wraps at 54 and its low 32 bits are the same residue. The down-cast and out are untouched, and the block's out[53:32] is read by nothing.
  • Power-up: every register the rewrite adds (elt_sum_n, nbbar, add_out_reg) and every register of the block is X until the first reset, and reset clears all of them; nothing relies on an initial value.

This is the same function as the kept candidate, which passed the reference simulation: there add_x+add_y+m is m ? elt_sum - acc : base + acc, add_out = m ? elt_sum : add_sum and base <= m ? elt_sum - acc : base. Here acc_sum = base + acc and ~nb_sum = (elt_sum - acc) - 1 = base_new - 1, with base held as nbbar + 1 and elt_sum held as ~elt_sum_n; the two differ only in gate grouping.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is unconditional, and in particular it does not assume anything about ready_for_eltwise_op or about reset preceding an eltwise load, which the reference testbench drives independently of each other.

Blocks, tiles

84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each taking a DSP tile alone: 84 tiles, the same count as the baseline (where the 84 two_mult_18x19 multiplies each sit alone on a tile, unpaired) and the same as the kept candidate. No tile is saved or spent.

Blocks in series, and the stages

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: a block's out feeds soft logic and a flip-flop, never another block. Its internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the baseline's 5.576 ns critical path, and the path into it (a PE register through in_a/in_b into a/b) is a register-to-first-block wire of 1.314 to 1.940 ns plus 0.019 setup, ~1.96 ns worst, also below.

The stage the rewrite leaves in soft logic, and the one that set the kept candidate's period, is from acc (block out) to add_out_reg/nbbar:

  • data: 0.060 (block clock-to-Q) + ~1.4 (block into soft logic, measured on this design's own routes) + 0.344 (33-bit chain) + 0.660 (wire) + 0.153 (mux + reset-gate LUT) + 0.019 (setup) ≈ 2.64 ns
  • control: the eltwise_add_mode cone reached the kept candidate's mux at 2.703 ns before the mux LUT; it now ends one LUT later, at 2.703 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.87 ns
  • elt_sum_n: PE register 0.060 + ~1.63 (register to this logic) + ~0.30 (17-bit chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.82 ns

So STAGE_NS 3.0, against the kept candidate's measured 5.42 ns and the baseline's 5.576 ns. The kept candidate's path had the late control signal and the chain and a second LUT level in series; this one has the control signal and one LUT level, or the chain and one LUT level, never both. Caveat stated plainly: the design's measured period is the worst stage anywhere in conv_layer, and the two baseline paths I was given (5.576 and 5.496 ns) were both qmult multiply stages that this rewrite removes; if some untouched stage elsewhere in the design sits above 3.0 ns the period will be set there, and the gain will come from area and wirelength alone.

What soft logic leaves which stage

Leaving (per instance, versus the unmodified design): the 16x16 multiply and the 32-bit accumulate add qadd, both now inside the block; a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_reg (96 flip-flops with their reset-gate LUTs); and the add_in1/add_in2 select layers (64 LUTs). Leaving versus the kept candidate: its add_x and add_y layers (64 LUTs, the two 32-bit mux layers that sat in front of the carry chain) — these are the stage that was slowest, so removing them is exactly where the delay comes from.

Entering (per instance): the 17-bit eltwise adder with its 17 reset/invert LUTs and 17 flip-flops; two 33-bit carry chains (~34 adder cells, up from ~17 in the kept candidate); the nbbar register (32 flip-flops) with its 32-LUT select layer; and the 32-LUT add_out select layer. Net against the kept candidate: about 64 fewer LUT-BLEs and about 17 more adder cells per instance, i.e. a smaller stage as well as a shorter one, so area and wirelength should not regress.

Register stages, before and after

Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> qadd -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4).
After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (stage 1) -> multiply and accumulate inside the block -> the block's out (stage 2) -> one soft adder and one LUT -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4). Same number of stages and the same cycle alignment: a product applied at cycle k reaches add_out_reg at edge k+3 in both. elt_sum_n and nbbar are stage-1 and stage-3 registers respectively on the eltwise path, replacing a_flopped/b_flopped and holding the accumulator offset; no stage is added or removed.

DESIGN_CHANGE: processing_element already holds the mac in reset whenever eltwise_mode is high and ready_for_eltwise_op is low (conv_layer.v:2647), so in the design's own use the accumulator is 0 at the first eltwise-add load. If seq_mac were specified so that an eltwise-add load may only occur while the block's running sum is 0 (for instance by gating eltwise_add with the first cycle after reset releases, or by holding mac_reset through the whole eltwise-add burst), the offset would be just the eltwise sum: the second carry chain, the 32-bit nbbar register and its 32-LUT select layer would all disappear, leaving one chain and one LUT layer per mac.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.

15	    input reset,

17	    input [26:0] b,

23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24

27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54

32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;

Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source and the reference files before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.0
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): clk -> clk; the seq_mac input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> wire acc, the running sum of this mac's products since its last reset; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept candidate (revision 2). The region converted, the block count, the tile count and the arithmetic computed are all unchanged; only the grouping of the soft correction logic changes, to take the late control signal off the carry chain and to delete two 32-bit LUT layers. The resulting seq_mac computes exactly the same function as the kept candidate (shown below by algebra), so it carries the same correctness risk as a candidate that already passed the reference simulation.

Candidate regions

There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances, and it is converted in all 84 instances. Nothing is left.

Why the kept candidate's stage is slow, and what this revision changes

The measured seed-1 path of the kept candidate is: a control register (lo10534) -> 2 LUT levels -> $mux~1192 (the add_x/add_y input mux) -> 33-bit carry chain -> n33606 (the add_out output mux + reset gate) -> $sdff. The control signal eltwise_add_mode reaches the input mux only at 2.857 ns (it comes from the slice's op register through two levels of decode), and the kept candidate then puts a whole carry chain plus a second LUT level behind it: 2.857 + 1.418 (wire) + 0.344 (chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 = 5.447 ns.

The fix is to take eltwise_add_mode out of the adder inputs entirely: use two adders with operands read straight out of registers (no LUT level in front of either chain), and let eltwise_add_mode appear only in the single LUT layer that sits between each chain and its flip-flop. Then the late control signal sees one LUT and a setup (~2.9 ns), and the data sees a chain and one LUT (~2.6 ns), instead of both in series.

The inversion needed for the subtract would otherwise re-introduce a LUT level in front of a chain, so both inverted values are stored inverted: the eltwise sum register holds ~(a+b) and the offset register holds ~(acc - elt_sum). Both inversions are then absorbed free of charge into LUT layers that already exist (the reset gate behind the 17-bit adder, and the two output mux layers), and both chains read acc, a register (nbbar) and a register (elt_sum_n) directly.

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance

Replace the body of seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) between the port/out/add_out declarations and the unchanged down-cast (mac_out, lines 2820-2844) with the following; keep reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;, the mac_out assignment and the out always block exactly as they are. Delete a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2, the qmult mult_u1 instance and the qadd add_u1 instance. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions in the file (they have no other instantiation; synthesis drops them). Change nothing outside seq_mac.

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of every product this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum. It is
//held inverted so the subtract below reads its operands straight out of registers
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum_n;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum_n <= {(`DWIDTH+1){1'b1}};
  end else begin
    elt_sum_n <= ~({1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b});
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ~elt_sum_n};

//nbbar = base - 1, base being this mac's offset from the block's running sum. Holding it
//decremented and inverted lets the sum below carry its +1 in at the bottom of the chain
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nbbar;

//base + acc, the running value. Both operands come from a register or the block, no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   acc_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {nbbar, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_sum     = acc_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//acc - elt_sum, the new offset on a load. ~elt_sum_ext is {1s, elt_sum_n}, again no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   nb_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}, elt_sum_n, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nb_sum     = nb_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : acc_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

//the offset only moves on a cycle that loads the eltwise sum
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    nbbar <= {(2*`DWIDTH){1'b1}};
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    nbbar <= ~nb_sum;
  end
end
Why it computes the original

Write af, bf, mr, ar for the original's a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, S for af+bf, m for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, and index by clock edge.

  • The block's operand registers obey areg(k) = reset(k) ? 0 : a(k-1), the same recurrence as af, because the block's reset is the module's reset and both are synchronous and clear to 0. So areg = af, breg = bf, and acc(k) - acc(k-1) = af(k-1)*bf(k-1) = mr(k) on every non-reset edge; on a reset edge both acc and ar are 0.
  • Invariant: ar(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) (mod 2^32), with base = nbbar + 1. Non-load edge: ar(k+1) = ar(k) + mr(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) + (acc(k)-acc(k-1)) = base(k) + acc(k), which is what acc_sum = acc + nbbar + 1 produces, and nbbar holds still. Load edge (m(k)): ar(k+1) = S_ext(k), taken straight from the mux input elt_sum_ext, and nbbar(k+1) = ~nb_sum(k) = ~(acc(k) - S_ext(k)), so base(k+1) = nbbar(k+1)+1 = S_ext(k) - acc(k) and the invariant holds again. Reset edge: ar = 0, acc = 0, base = 0, elt_sum = 0, and the cycle after reset gives m ? 0 : 0+0 = 0, matching the original, whose mr and ar are also 0 there.
  • ~elt_sum_n is exactly af+bf (17 bits, zero-extended to 32 as in the original {{DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} form); on a reset edge ~{17{1'b1}} = 0, matching the original's cleared af,bf`.
  • nbbar resets to all ones, i.e. base = 0, matching ar = 0 with acc = 0.
  • All arithmetic is unsigned and wraps at 32 bits in both versions; the block wraps at 54 and its low 32 bits are the same residue. The down-cast and out are untouched, and the block's out[53:32] is read by nothing.
  • Power-up: every register the rewrite adds (elt_sum_n, nbbar, add_out_reg) and every register of the block is X until the first reset, and reset clears all of them; nothing relies on an initial value.

This is the same function as the kept candidate, which passed the reference simulation: there add_x+add_y+m is m ? elt_sum - acc : base + acc, add_out = m ? elt_sum : add_sum and base <= m ? elt_sum - acc : base. Here acc_sum = base + acc and ~nb_sum = (elt_sum - acc) - 1 = base_new - 1, with base held as nbbar + 1 and elt_sum held as ~elt_sum_n; the two differ only in gate grouping.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is unconditional, and in particular it does not assume anything about ready_for_eltwise_op or about reset preceding an eltwise load, which the reference testbench drives independently of each other.

Blocks, tiles

84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each taking a DSP tile alone: 84 tiles, the same count as the baseline (where the 84 two_mult_18x19 multiplies each sit alone on a tile, unpaired) and the same as the kept candidate. No tile is saved or spent.

Blocks in series, and the stages

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: a block's out feeds soft logic and a flip-flop, never another block. Its internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the baseline's 5.576 ns critical path, and the path into it (a PE register through in_a/in_b into a/b) is a register-to-first-block wire of 1.314 to 1.940 ns plus 0.019 setup, ~1.96 ns worst, also below.

The stage the rewrite leaves in soft logic, and the one that set the kept candidate's period, is from acc (block out) to add_out_reg/nbbar:

  • data: 0.060 (block clock-to-Q) + ~1.4 (block into soft logic, measured on this design's own routes) + 0.344 (33-bit chain) + 0.660 (wire) + 0.153 (mux + reset-gate LUT) + 0.019 (setup) ≈ 2.64 ns
  • control: the eltwise_add_mode cone reached the kept candidate's mux at 2.703 ns before the mux LUT; it now ends one LUT later, at 2.703 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.87 ns
  • elt_sum_n: PE register 0.060 + ~1.63 (register to this logic) + ~0.30 (17-bit chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.82 ns

So STAGE_NS 3.0, against the kept candidate's measured 5.42 ns and the baseline's 5.576 ns. The kept candidate's path had the late control signal and the chain and a second LUT level in series; this one has the control signal and one LUT level, or the chain and one LUT level, never both. Caveat stated plainly: the design's measured period is the worst stage anywhere in conv_layer, and the two baseline paths I was given (5.576 and 5.496 ns) were both qmult multiply stages that this rewrite removes; if some untouched stage elsewhere in the design sits above 3.0 ns the period will be set there, and the gain will come from area and wirelength alone.

What soft logic leaves which stage

Leaving (per instance, versus the unmodified design): the 16x16 multiply and the 32-bit accumulate add qadd, both now inside the block; a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_reg (96 flip-flops with their reset-gate LUTs); and the add_in1/add_in2 select layers (64 LUTs). Leaving versus the kept candidate: its add_x and add_y layers (64 LUTs, the two 32-bit mux layers that sat in front of the carry chain) — these are the stage that was slowest, so removing them is exactly where the delay comes from.

Entering (per instance): the 17-bit eltwise adder with its 17 reset/invert LUTs and 17 flip-flops; two 33-bit carry chains (~34 adder cells, up from ~17 in the kept candidate); the nbbar register (32 flip-flops) with its 32-LUT select layer; and the 32-LUT add_out select layer. Net against the kept candidate: about 64 fewer LUT-BLEs and about 17 more adder cells per instance, i.e. a smaller stage as well as a shorter one, so area and wirelength should not regress.

Register stages, before and after

Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> qadd -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4).
After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (stage 1) -> multiply and accumulate inside the block -> the block's out (stage 2) -> one soft adder and one LUT -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4). Same number of stages and the same cycle alignment: a product applied at cycle k reaches add_out_reg at edge k+3 in both. elt_sum_n and nbbar are stage-1 and stage-3 registers respectively on the eltwise path, replacing a_flopped/b_flopped and holding the accumulator offset; no stage is added or removed.

The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

Region qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) — module seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765)

Replaced the body between the out/add_out declarations and the unchanged down-cast (formerly lines 2777–2818) exactly as the plan specifies:

  • Deleted a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2, the qmult mult_u1 instance and the qadd add_u1 instance. The qmult and qadd module definitions remain in the file, now uninstantiated.
  • Instantiated mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk with .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the module's own reset, which at each PE is mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), .a/.b as the 16-bit inputs zero-extended to 27, and .out(mac_acc); acc is mac_acc[31:0], mac_acc[53:32] is unused. The block's operand registers stand in for a_flopped/b_flopped and its running sum carries the accumulated products.
  • Added the soft logic the block cannot do, in the plan's grouping: elt_sum_n (the 17-bit eltwise sum held inverted), nbbar (the accumulator offset, held decremented and inverted, loaded only when eltwise_add_mode), the two 33-bit sums acc_sum = acc + nbbar + 1 and nb_sum = acc - elt_sum_ext with their carry-ins at the bottom of the chain, the add_out select, and add_out_reg with its synchronous clear.
  • Kept reg [15:0] out;, wire [31:0] add_out;, the mac_out down-cast and the out always block untouched. Nothing outside seq_mac changed.

No ASSUMES: line — the rewrite is unconditional.

I also ran the reference testbench myself (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v with mac_int_27x27.v, against the original seq_mac renamed as the reference): pass on the default seed, three other seeds, and the START_ONLY build — 0 mismatches in 1156 checked cycles each, no X on the DUT only.

module seq_mac(a, b, out, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset, clk);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
input eltwise_mode;
input eltwise_add;
input reset;
input clk;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out;
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), .o_result(mul_out));

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    mul_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    mul_out_reg <= mul_out;
  end
end


wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_in1;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_in2;
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
assign add_in1 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} : add_out_reg;
assign add_in2 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, b_flopped} : mul_out_reg;

qadd add_u1(.a(add_in1), .b(add_in2), .c(add_out));

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out = 
    (add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
        (
           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
        )
        : //negative number
        (
           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
        );


always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    out <= 0;
  end else begin
    out <= mac_out;
  end
end

endmodule


module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplicand;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplier;
output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] o_result;

assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;
//DW02_mult #(`DWIDTH,`DWIDTH) u_mult(.A(i_multiplicand), .B(i_multiplier), .TC(1'b1), .PRODUCT(o_result));

endmodule

module qadd(a,b,c);
input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] c;

assign c = a + b;
//DW01_add #(`DWIDTH) u_add(.A(a), .B(b), .CI(1'b0), .SUM(c), .CO());
endmodule

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
===
// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_27x27 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [26:0] a,
    input [26:0] b,
    output reg [53:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [26:0] a_reg;
    reg [26:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin

 "new_string": "//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,\n//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the\n//sum of every product this mac has accumulated since the last reset\nwire [53:0] mac_acc;\nmac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),\n                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),\n                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),\n                        .out(mac_acc));\nwire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];\n\nwire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;\n\n//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing\n//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum.         if (nrec == 1 && mm[j] == 1 && same == 1) $display("first divergence: cycle=%0d t=%0t phase=%0s output=%0s ref=%0h dut=%0h", cycle, $time, phase, oname(j), e, a);
    end endtask
    // the compare, at the negedge before the stimulus of the next cycle is applied
    task check; begin
        cycle = cycle + 1;
        if (cmp) begin
            n = n + 1;
            if (out_dut !== out_ref) note_mismatch(0, out_ref, out_dut);
            if (out_dut !== prev[0][15:0]) begin delsum = delsum + 1; dl[0] = dl[0] + 1; end
            if (^out_ref === 1'bx) xr[0] = xr[0] + 1; else begin if (^out_dut === 1'bx) xd[0] = xd[0] + 1; if (out_ref != 0) act[0] = act[0] + 1; end
        end
        prev[0] = out_ref;
    end endtask
    // one cycle: compare, then the caller sets the next inputs
    task tick; begin @(negedge clk); check; end endtask
    task set_data;   // every free input random over its full width; controls by their probability
    begin
        draw; a[15:0] = r32[15:0];
        draw; b[15:0] = r32[15:0];
        draw; eltwise_mode = ((r32 % 8) < 1);
        draw; eltwise_add = ((r32 % 4) < 3);
    end endtask
    task set_zero; begin   // the zero phases: every free input 0, controls at their idle value
        a = 0;
        b = 0;
        eltwise_mode = 0;
        eltwise_add = 0;
    end endtask
    task resets_on; begin reset = 1'b1; end endtask
    task resets_off; begin reset = 1'b0; end endtask
    task resets_random; begin
        draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % `RESET_RATE) == 0) ? 1'b1 : 1'b0;
    end endtask
    task set_random; begin set_data; resets_random; end endtask
    task rand_cycles; input integer m; integer q; begin for (q = 0; q < m; q = q + 1) begin tick; set_data; resets_off; end end endtask
    task zero_cycles; input integer m; integer q; begin for (q = 0; q < m; q = q + 1) begin tick; set_zero; resets_off; end end endtask
    task impulse_probe; integer q; begin
        phase = "imp-zero"; zero_cycles(`LEAD_IN);
        phase = "impulse"; tick; set_zero; resets_off; a = 1; b = 1;
        phase = "imp-zero";
        for (q = 0; q < 6; q = q + 1) begin tick; set_zero; resets_off;
            $display("impulse+%0d cycles after apply: out ref=%0h dut=%0h", q + 1, out_ref, out_dut);
        end
    end endtask

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[27], b[27]
  • output ports: out[54]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 54 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 54 bits (modulo 2^54), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the source and the reference files before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 3.0
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): clk -> clk; the seq_mac input reset (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647) -> reset; {11'b0, a} (module input a, zero-extended 16->27) -> a; {11'b0, b} (module input b, zero-extended 16->27) -> b; out[31:0] -> wire acc, the running sum of this mac's products since its last reset; out[53:32] unused.

PLAN:

This is a revision of the kept candidate (revision 2). The region converted, the block count, the tile count and the arithmetic computed are all unchanged; only the grouping of the soft correction logic changes, to take the late control signal off the carry chain and to delete two 32-bit LUT layers. The resulting seq_mac computes exactly the same function as the kept candidate (shown below by algebra), so it carries the same correctness risk as a candidate that already passed the reference simulation.

Candidate regions

There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances, and it is converted in all 84 instances. Nothing is left.

Why the kept candidate's stage is slow, and what this revision changes

The measured seed-1 path of the kept candidate is: a control register (lo10534) -> 2 LUT levels -> $mux~1192 (the add_x/add_y input mux) -> 33-bit carry chain -> n33606 (the add_out output mux + reset gate) -> $sdff. The control signal eltwise_add_mode reaches the input mux only at 2.857 ns (it comes from the slice's op register through two levels of decode), and the kept candidate then puts a whole carry chain plus a second LUT level behind it: 2.857 + 1.418 (wire) + 0.344 (chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 = 5.447 ns.

The fix is to take eltwise_add_mode out of the adder inputs entirely: use two adders with operands read straight out of registers (no LUT level in front of either chain), and let eltwise_add_mode appear only in the single LUT layer that sits between each chain and its flip-flop. Then the late control signal sees one LUT and a setup (~2.9 ns), and the data sees a chain and one LUT (~2.6 ns), instead of both in series.

The inversion needed for the subtract would otherwise re-introduce a LUT level in front of a chain, so both inverted values are stored inverted: the eltwise sum register holds ~(a+b) and the offset register holds ~(acc - elt_sum). Both inversions are then absorbed free of charge into LUT layers that already exist (the reset gate behind the 17-bit adder, and the two output mux layers), and both chains read acc, a register (nbbar) and a register (elt_sum_n) directly.

The rewrite, per seq_mac instance

Replace the body of seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) between the port/out/add_out declarations and the unchanged down-cast (mac_out, lines 2820-2844) with the following; keep reg [DWIDTH-1:0] out;, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;, the mac_out assignment and the out always block exactly as they are. Delete a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_in1, add_in2, the qmult mult_u1 instance and the qadd add_u1 instance. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions in the file (they have no other instantiation; synthesis drops them). Change nothing outside seq_mac.

verilog
//the block registers a and b itself, on the same clock and with the same synchronous clear,
//so its operand registers stand in for a_flopped and b_flopped, and its running sum holds the
//sum of every product this mac has accumulated since the last reset
wire [53:0] mac_acc;
mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                        .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
                        .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
                        .out(mac_acc));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum. It is
//held inverted so the subtract below reads its operands straight out of registers
reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum_n;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_sum_n <= {(`DWIDTH+1){1'b1}};
  end else begin
    elt_sum_n <= ~({1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b});
  end
end
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, ~elt_sum_n};

//nbbar = base - 1, base being this mac's offset from the block's running sum. Holding it
//decremented and inverted lets the sum below carry its +1 in at the bottom of the chain
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nbbar;

//base + acc, the running value. Both operands come from a register or the block, no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   acc_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {nbbar, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_sum     = acc_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

//acc - elt_sum, the new offset on a load. ~elt_sum_ext is {1s, elt_sum_n}, again no logic ahead
wire [2*`DWIDTH:0]   nb_sum_ext = {acc, 1'b1} + {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}, elt_sum_n, 1'b1};
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] nb_sum     = nb_sum_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];

assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : acc_sum;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    add_out_reg <= 0;
  end else begin
    add_out_reg <= add_out;
  end
end

//the offset only moves on a cycle that loads the eltwise sum
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    nbbar <= {(2*`DWIDTH){1'b1}};
  end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
    nbbar <= ~nb_sum;
  end
end
Why it computes the original

Write af, bf, mr, ar for the original's a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, S for af+bf, m for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, and index by clock edge.

  • The block's operand registers obey areg(k) = reset(k) ? 0 : a(k-1), the same recurrence as af, because the block's reset is the module's reset and both are synchronous and clear to 0. So areg = af, breg = bf, and acc(k) - acc(k-1) = af(k-1)*bf(k-1) = mr(k) on every non-reset edge; on a reset edge both acc and ar are 0.
  • Invariant: ar(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) (mod 2^32), with base = nbbar + 1. Non-load edge: ar(k+1) = ar(k) + mr(k) = base(k) + acc(k-1) + (acc(k)-acc(k-1)) = base(k) + acc(k), which is what acc_sum = acc + nbbar + 1 produces, and nbbar holds still. Load edge (m(k)): ar(k+1) = S_ext(k), taken straight from the mux input elt_sum_ext, and nbbar(k+1) = ~nb_sum(k) = ~(acc(k) - S_ext(k)), so base(k+1) = nbbar(k+1)+1 = S_ext(k) - acc(k) and the invariant holds again. Reset edge: ar = 0, acc = 0, base = 0, elt_sum = 0, and the cycle after reset gives m ? 0 : 0+0 = 0, matching the original, whose mr and ar are also 0 there.
  • ~elt_sum_n is exactly af+bf (17 bits, zero-extended to 32 as in the original {{DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} form); on a reset edge ~{17{1'b1}} = 0, matching the original's cleared af,bf`.
  • nbbar resets to all ones, i.e. base = 0, matching ar = 0 with acc = 0.
  • All arithmetic is unsigned and wraps at 32 bits in both versions; the block wraps at 54 and its low 32 bits are the same residue. The down-cast and out are untouched, and the block's out[53:32] is read by nothing.
  • Power-up: every register the rewrite adds (elt_sum_n, nbbar, add_out_reg) and every register of the block is X until the first reset, and reset clears all of them; nothing relies on an initial value.

This is the same function as the kept candidate, which passed the reference simulation: there add_x+add_y+m is m ? elt_sum - acc : base + acc, add_out = m ? elt_sum : add_sum and base <= m ? elt_sum - acc : base. Here acc_sum = base + acc and ~nb_sum = (elt_sum - acc) - 1 = base_new - 1, with base held as nbbar + 1 and elt_sum held as ~elt_sum_n; the two differ only in gate grouping.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is unconditional, and in particular it does not assume anything about ready_for_eltwise_op or about reset preceding an eltwise load, which the reference testbench drives independently of each other.

Blocks, tiles

84 blocks, one per seq_mac, each taking a DSP tile alone: 84 tiles, the same count as the baseline (where the 84 two_mult_18x19 multiplies each sit alone on a tile, unpaired) and the same as the kept candidate. No tile is saved or spent.

Blocks in series, and the stages

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: a block's out feeds soft logic and a flip-flop, never another block. Its internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the baseline's 5.576 ns critical path, and the path into it (a PE register through in_a/in_b into a/b) is a register-to-first-block wire of 1.314 to 1.940 ns plus 0.019 setup, ~1.96 ns worst, also below.

The stage the rewrite leaves in soft logic, and the one that set the kept candidate's period, is from acc (block out) to add_out_reg/nbbar:

  • data: 0.060 (block clock-to-Q) + ~1.4 (block into soft logic, measured on this design's own routes) + 0.344 (33-bit chain) + 0.660 (wire) + 0.153 (mux + reset-gate LUT) + 0.019 (setup) ≈ 2.64 ns
  • control: the eltwise_add_mode cone reached the kept candidate's mux at 2.703 ns before the mux LUT; it now ends one LUT later, at 2.703 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.87 ns
  • elt_sum_n: PE register 0.060 + ~1.63 (register to this logic) + ~0.30 (17-bit chain) + 0.660 + 0.153 + 0.019 ≈ 2.82 ns

So STAGE_NS 3.0, against the kept candidate's measured 5.42 ns and the baseline's 5.576 ns. The kept candidate's path had the late control signal and the chain and a second LUT level in series; this one has the control signal and one LUT level, or the chain and one LUT level, never both. Caveat stated plainly: the design's measured period is the worst stage anywhere in conv_layer, and the two baseline paths I was given (5.576 and 5.496 ns) were both qmult multiply stages that this rewrite removes; if some untouched stage elsewhere in the design sits above 3.0 ns the period will be set there, and the gain will come from area and wirelength alone.

What soft logic leaves which stage

Leaving (per instance, versus the unmodified design): the 16x16 multiply and the 32-bit accumulate add qadd, both now inside the block; a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_reg (96 flip-flops with their reset-gate LUTs); and the add_in1/add_in2 select layers (64 LUTs). Leaving versus the kept candidate: its add_x and add_y layers (64 LUTs, the two 32-bit mux layers that sat in front of the carry chain) — these are the stage that was slowest, so removing them is exactly where the delay comes from.

Entering (per instance): the 17-bit eltwise adder with its 17 reset/invert LUTs and 17 flip-flops; two 33-bit carry chains (~34 adder cells, up from ~17 in the kept candidate); the nbbar register (32 flip-flops) with its 32-LUT select layer; and the 32-LUT add_out select layer. Net against the kept candidate: about 64 fewer LUT-BLEs and about 17 more adder cells per instance, i.e. a smaller stage as well as a shorter one, so area and wirelength should not regress.

Register stages, before and after

Before: a,b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> qmult -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> qadd -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4).
After: a,b -> the block's operand registers (stage 1) -> multiply and accumulate inside the block -> the block's out (stage 2) -> one soft adder and one LUT -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4). Same number of stages and the same cycle alignment: a product applied at cycle k reaches add_out_reg at edge k+3 in both. elt_sum_n and nbbar are stage-1 and stage-3 registers respectively on the eltwise path, replacing a_flopped/b_flopped and holding the accumulator offset; no stage is added or removed.

DESIGN_CHANGE: processing_element already holds the mac in reset whenever eltwise_mode is high and ready_for_eltwise_op is low (conv_layer.v:2647), so in the design's own use the accumulator is 0 at the first eltwise-add load. If seq_mac were specified so that an eltwise-add load may only occur while the block's running sum is 0 (for instance by gating eltwise_add with the first cycle after reset releases, or by holding mac_reset through the whole eltwise-add burst), the offset would be just the eltwise sum: the second carry chain, the 32-bit nbbar register and its 32-LUT select layer would all disappear, leaving one chain and one LUT layer per mac.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the contract and the rules:

Plan conformance — The edit touches only seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2774-2832); qmult/qadd module definitions (2863-2879), the down-cast (2837-2849) and the out register (2852-2858) are untouched, and the run log's subtree check confirms everything outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac is unchanged. The inserted body is literally the plan's code block, in the plan's order.

Ports — All four block inputs connected by name at 2781-2784: .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the module port, i.e. the PE's mac_reset, 2647), .a({11'b0, a}), .b({11'b0, b}) with DWIDTH=16 so 27-DWIDTH=11, both 27 bits. out drives mac_acc; acc takes [31:0] (2785) and [53:32] is read by nothing, as planned.

Same behavior — With af(k)=reset_k?0:a_k etc., the block model (sim_reference/mac_int_27x27.v:29-38) gives a_reg=af, b_reg=bf, and acc(k)-acc(k-1)=af(k-1)*bf(k-1)=mr(k) mod 2^32 on non-reset edges, 0 on reset edges. ~elt_sum_n(k) equals af(k)+bf(k) including the reset edge (~{17{1'b1}}=0). The invariant add_out_reg(k) = base(k)+acc(k-1), base=nbbar+1, holds by induction over the three cases (hold, eltwise load, reset), with the reset edge re-establishing ar=base=acc=0; so add_out_reg matches the original's ar on every cycle and the untouched down-cast gives the same out. The {x,1'b1}+{y,1'b1} then [32:1] idiom yields x+y+1 mod 2^32 correctly despite the 33-bit truncation (bit 0 is always 0), and {15{1'b1},elt_sum_n} is exactly ~elt_sum_ext, so nb_sum = acc - elt_sum_ext. Unsigned 16×16 products fit in 32 bits, and truncating the block's mod-2^54 sum to 32 bits gives the same residue as the original's mod-2^32 accumulate.

Visibility / terms — a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg were local to seq_mac with no hierarchical readers; the one value still needed outside the block (af+bf) is recomputed in soft logic, which is allowed. Both original terms (the product accumulate and the eltwise sum) are preserved.

Registers and reset — The operand registers map one-for-one onto the block's (same clock, synchronous clear to 0, no enable); the block's reset is the module's reset, so behavior matches after mid-run resets, not just at start-up (the reset_between_phases diagnostic also passed). elt_sum_n, nbbar and add_out_reg all start X and are cleared by reset; nothing relies on a power-up value. No stage is added or removed.

Assumptions — None claimed and none needed; the rewrite is unconditional and does not depend on ready_for_eltwise_op or on the accumulator being 0 at an eltwise load.
