output
1	// mac_int_27x27: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^54 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[27], b[27] in; out[54] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_27x27 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [26:0] a,
17	    input [26:0] b,
18	    output reg [53:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [26:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [26:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [53:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 27x27 -> 54, exact
27	    wire [53:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^54
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

+mac_int_27x27 u_mac_blk(.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
+ .a({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a}),
+ .b({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b}),
+ .out(mac_acc));
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = mac_acc[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];
+
+wire eltwise_add_mode = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
+
+//the eltwise sum, the one add the block cannot do. a_flopped and b_flopped were read by nothing
+//else, so the add moves ahead of the register and the pair of them becomes this one sum
+reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum;
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) begin
- a_flopped <= 0;
- b_flopped <= 0;
+ elt_sum <= 0;
end else begin
- a_flopped <= a;
- b_flopped <= b;
+ elt_sum <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
end
end
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_sum_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_sum};
-qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), .o_result(mul_out));
+//the offset of this mac's accumulator from the block's running sum. It only moves on a cycle
+//that loads the eltwise sum, where it becomes elt_sum - acc
+reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] base;
+
+//one adder for both, they are never needed on the same cycle: base + acc normally,
+//elt_sum - acc on a load. The 33rd bit carries the +1 of the subtract, so this stays one chain
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_x = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : base;
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_y = eltwise_add_mode ? ~acc : acc;
+wire [2*`DWIDTH:0] add_ext = {add_x, 1'b1} + {add_y, eltwise_add_mode};
+wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_sum = add_ext[2*`DWIDTH:1];
+assign add_out = eltwise_add_mode ? elt_sum_ext : add_sum;
+
+reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) begin
- mul_out_reg <= 0;
+ add_out_reg <= 0;
end else begin
- mul_out_reg <= mul_out;
+ add_out_reg <= add_out;
end
end
-
-wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_in1;
-wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_in2;
-reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
-assign add_in1 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} : add_out_reg;
-assign add_in2 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, b_flopped} : mul_out_reg;
-
-qadd add_u1(.a(add_in1), .b(add_in2), .c(add_out));
-
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) begin
- add_out_reg <= 0;
- end else begin
- add_out_reg <= add_out;
+ base <= 0;
+ end else if (eltwise_add_mode) begin
+ base <= add_sum;
end
end

Objective loop

4 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 3
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -10.90% against Pass 3, min-gain 0.0581 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac))
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 6.1312e+07 · critical path 6.32294 ns · wirelength 294638 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 1.00726e+14 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 5.81%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 5.81%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
